&\times(-1)^{np-1}e^{-\frac{2l}{p}\pi\sqrt{-1}}\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{(l+p/2)^{2}}{p}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]+O(n^{-3/2}).\end{split}

We finally have

\begin{split}&I_{2,1}\\
=&\sum_{-p/2-1/n<l<p/2-1/n}\operatorname{Res}(\psi_{2};\xi_{2,l})\int_{C_{\theta}+w_{2}}\varphi(z_{2})e^{nF(\xi_{2,l},z_{2})}\,dz_{2}\\
=&-2\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}-p/2-1/n<l<p/2-1/n\\
\frac{2ab}{p}(1+p/2+1/n)<m^{\prime}<2ab\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Res}(\psi_{2};\xi_{2,l})\operatorname{Res}(\varphi;\eta_{m^{\prime}})e^{nF(\xi_{2,l},\eta_{m^{\prime}})}\\
&+\sum_{-p/2-1/n<l<p/2-1/n}\operatorname{Res}(\psi_{2};\xi_{2,l})\int_{C_{\theta}+\alpha+\beta/n}\varphi(z_{2})e^{nF(\xi_{2,l},z_{2})}\,dz_{2}\\
=&-2\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}-p/2-1/n<l<p/2-1/n\\
\frac{2ab}{p}(1+p/2+1/n)<m^{\prime}<2ab\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Res}(\psi_{2};\xi_{2,l})\operatorname{Res}(\varphi;\eta_{m^{\prime}})e^{nF(\xi_{2,l},\eta_{m^{\prime}})}\\
&+\sum_{-p/2-1/n<l<p/2-1/n}\sqrt{\frac{ab(p-ab)\pi^{2}\sqrt{-1}}{pn}}(-1)^{ab+a+b}\sqrt{-1}\frac{\sin\left(\frac{2al\pi}{p}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2bl\pi}{p}\right)}{\sin\left(\frac{2abl\pi}{p}\right)}e^{-\frac{1}{p-ab}\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\\
&\quad\times(-1)^{np-1}e^{-\frac{2l}{p}\pi\sqrt{-1}}\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{(l+p/2)^{2}}{p}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]+O(n^{-3/2})\end{split}

where
\frac{2ab}{p}(l+p/2+1/n)<m^{\prime}<2ab
.

5.4.
The sum over
m
in
V_{2}

In this subsection, we study the asymptotic behavior of
I_{2,2}
.

Consider

\int_{C_{\theta}+w_{1}}\psi_{2}(z_{1})e^{nF(z_{1},\eta_{m})}\,dz_{1}.

The polynomial
F(z_{1},\eta_{m})=-\frac{(z_{1}-\eta_{m})^{2}}{(p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}}-\frac{\eta_{m}^{2}}{ab\pi\sqrt{-1}}+2z_{1}
has a unique critical point

\gamma:=(m/2+p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}

and

F(z_{1},\eta_{m})=F(\gamma,\eta_{m})-\frac{1}{(p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}}(z_{1}-\gamma)^{2}.

Note that
\gamma
is not a pole of
\psi_{2}(z_{1})
.
We have

(18)

\begin{split}&\int_{C_{\theta}+w_{1}}\psi_{2}(z_{1})e^{nF(z_{1},\eta_{m})}\,dz_{1}\\
=&\int_{C_{\theta}+\gamma}\psi_{2}(z_{1})e^{nF(z_{1},\eta_{m})}-2\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{m/2+p/2-ab-1/n<l^{\prime}<p/2-1/n}\operatorname{Res}(\psi_{2};\xi_{2,l^{\prime}})e^{nF(\xi_{2,l^{\prime}},\eta_{m})}\,dz_{1}.\end{split}

Note that

\begin{split}\int_{C_{\theta}+\gamma}\psi_{2}(z_{1})e^{nF(z_{1},\eta_{m})}\,dz_{1}&=e^{nF(\gamma,\eta_{m})}\int_{C_{\theta}+\gamma}\psi_{2}(z_{1})e^{-\frac{n(z_{1}-\gamma)^{2}}{(p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}}}\,dz_{1}\\
&=e^{nF(\gamma,\eta_{m})}\int_{C_{\theta}}\psi_{2}(z_{1}+\gamma)e^{-\frac{nz_{1}^{2}}{(p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}}}\,dz_{1}.\end{split}

Since
\gamma=(m/2+p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}
and
\eta_{m}=m\pi\sqrt{-1}/2
, we have

F(\gamma,\eta_{m})=\left(p-ab-\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}+m\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}.

Note that
\psi_{2}(z_{1}+\zeta)=\coth(z_{1}+\gamma-\tilde{h}^{\prime})
.
Put
\delta:=(m/2+p/2-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}
.
Then
\psi_{2}(z_{1}+\gamma)=\coth(z_{1}+\delta-h).

The integral above becomes

\begin{split}\int_{C_{\theta}}\cot(z_{1}+\delta-h)e^{\frac{-nz_{1}^{2}}{(p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}}}\,dz_{1}&=\int_{C_{\theta}-h}\coth(z_{1}+\delta)e^{\frac{-n(z_{1}+h)^{2}}{(p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}}}\,dz_{1}\\
&=e^{-\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\int_{C_{\theta}-h}e^{\frac{-2z_{1}}{p-ab}}\coth(z_{1}+\delta)e^{\frac{-nz_{1}^{2}}{(p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}}}\,dz_{1}.\end{split}

There are two cases to consider.

Case 1:

If
m\neq p\pmod{2}
, then
\delta\in(\mathbb{Z}+1/2)\pi\sqrt{-1}
and so
\coth(z_{1}+\delta)=\coth(z_{1}+\pi\sqrt{-1}/2)
.
Hence we have

\begin{split}\int_{C_{\theta}-h}e^{\frac{-2z_{1}}{p-ab}}\coth(z_{1}+\delta)e^{\frac{-nz_{1}^{2}}{(p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}}}\,dz_{1}&=\int_{C_{\theta}}e^{\frac{-2z_{1}}{p-ab}}\coth(z_{1}+\pi\sqrt{-1}/2)e^{\frac{-nz_{1}^{2}}{(p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}}}\,dz_{1}\\
&=\sqrt{\frac{(p-ab)\pi^{2}\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\coth(\pi\sqrt{-1}/2)+O(n^{-3/2})\\
&=O(n^{-3/2})\end{split}

since there are no poles of
\coth(z_{1}+\pi\sqrt{-1}/2)
between
C_{\theta}
and
C_{\theta_{1}}-h
.

Case 2:

If
m=p\pmod{2}
, then
\delta\in\mathbb{Z}\pi\sqrt{-1}
and
\coth(z_{1}+\delta)=\coth(z_{1})
.
Hence we have

\begin{split}&\int_{C_{\theta}-h}e^{\frac{-2z_{1}}{p-ab}}\coth(z_{1}+\delta)e^{\frac{-nz_{1}^{2}}{(p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}}}\,dz_{1}\\
=&\int_{C_{\theta}-h}\left(e^{\frac{-2z_{1}}{p-ab}}\coth(z_{1})-\frac{1}{z_{1}}\right)e^{\frac{-nz_{1}^{2}}{(p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}}}\,dz_{1}+\int_{C_{\theta}-h}\frac{1}{z_{1}}e^{\frac{-nz_{1}^{2}}{(p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}}}\,dz_{1}\\
=&\int_{C_{\theta}}\left(e^{\frac{-2z_{1}}{p-ab}}\coth(z_{1})-\frac{1}{z_{1}}\right)e^{\frac{-nz_{1}^{2}}{(p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}}}\,dz_{1}+\int_{C_{\theta}-h}\frac{1}{z_{1}}e^{\frac{-nz_{1}^{2}}{(p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}}}\,dz_{1}\\
=&\sqrt{\frac{(p-ab)\pi^{2}\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\left(-\frac{2}{p-ab}\right)+O(n^{-3/2})+\int_{C_{\theta}-h}\frac{1}{z_{1}}e^{\frac{-nz_{1}^{2}}{(p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}}}\,dz_{1}\end{split}

since there are no poles of
e^{\frac{-2z_{1}}{p-ab}}\coth(z_{1})-\frac{1}{z_{1}}
between
C_{\theta}
and
C_{\theta}-h
.
Note that
\left(e^{\frac{-2z_{1}}{p-ab}}\coth(z_{1})-\frac{1}{z_{1}}\right)\Bigm|_{z_{1}=0}=\frac{-2}{p-ab}
.
Moreover we have

\int_{C_{\theta}-h}\frac{1}{z}e^{\frac{-nz^{2}}{(p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}}}\,dz=-\int_{C_{\theta}+h}\frac{1}{z}e^{\frac{-nz^{2}}{(p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}}}\,dz=\pi\sqrt{-1}.

Therefore in this case we have

\int_{C_{\theta}-h}e^{\frac{-2z_{1}}{p-ab}}\cosh(z_{1}+\delta)e^{\frac{-nz_{1}^{2}}{(p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}}}\,dz_{1}=\pi\sqrt{-1}-\frac{2}{p-ab}\sqrt{\frac{(p-ab)\pi^{2}\sqrt{-1}}{n}}+O(n^{-3/2})

and

\begin{split}&\int_{C_{\theta_{1}+\zeta}}\psi_{2}(z_{1})e^{nF(z_{1},\eta_{m})}\,dz_{1}\\
=&e^{nF(\gamma,\eta_{m})}e^{\frac{-\pi\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\int_{C_{\theta}-h}e^{\frac{-2z_{1}}{p-ab}}\coth(z_{1}+\delta)e^{\frac{-nz_{1}^{2}}{(p-ab)\pi\sqrt{-1}}}\,dz_{1}\\
=&\left(\pi\sqrt{-1}-2\sqrt{\frac{\pi^{2}\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\right)e^{-\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\exp\left[n\left(p-ab-\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}+m\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]+O(n^{-3/2}).\end{split}

Hence we have

\begin{split}I_{2,2}&=\sum_{m=1}^{2ab-1}\operatorname{Res}(\varphi;\eta_{m})\int_{C_{\theta}+w_{1}}\psi_{2}(z_{1})e^{nF(z_{1},\eta_{m})}\,dz_{1}\\
&=-2\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m/2+p/2-ab-1/n<l^{\prime}<p/2-1/n\\
1\leq m\leq 2ab-1\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Res}(\varphi;\eta_{m})\operatorname{Res}(\psi_{2};\zeta_{2,l^{\prime}})e^{nF(\zeta_{2,l^{\prime}},\eta_{m})}\\
&\quad+\sum_{m=1}^{2ab-1}\operatorname{Res}(\varphi;\eta_{m})\int_{C_{\theta}+\gamma}\psi_{2}(z_{1})e^{nF(z_{1},\eta_{m})}\,dz_{1}\\
&=-2\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m/2+p/2-ab-1/n<l^{\prime}<p/2-1/n\\
1\leq m\leq 2ab-1\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Res}(\varphi;\eta_{m})\operatorname{Res}(\psi_{2};\zeta_{2,l^{\prime}})e^{nF(\zeta_{2,l^{\prime}},\eta_{m})}\\
&\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m\leq 2ab-1\\
\text{$m-p$: even}\end{subarray}}(-1)(2\pi\sqrt{-1})\frac{(-1)^{m}}{2}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\\
&\quad\times\left(\pi\sqrt{-1}-2\sqrt{\frac{\pi^{2}\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\right)e^{-\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\exp\left[n\left(-ab-\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]+O(n^{-3/2}).\end{split}

5.5.
Summary

Now we combine the results so far.

Note that
I_{2,3}
is the sum of
\operatorname{Res}(\psi_{2};\xi_{2,l})\operatorname{Res}(\varphi;\eta_{m})e^{nF(\xi_{2,l},\eta_{m})}
over
(l,m)
.
The same summand appears both in (
17
) and in (
18
).
Taking cancellation into account, the range of summation turns out to be

\begin{split}&\mathcal{S}_{2}\\
:=&\left\{(l,m)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\bigm|-\frac{p}{2}<l+\frac{1}{n}<\frac{p}{2},0<m<2ab,a\nmid m,b\nmid m\right\}\\
&\setminus\left\{(l,m)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\bigm|-\frac{p}{2}<l+\frac{1}{n}<\frac{p}{2},\frac{2ab}{p}(l+\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{n})<m<2ab\right\}\\
&\setminus\left\{(l,m)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\Bigm|\frac{m}{2}+p-ab<l+\frac{p}{2}+\frac{1}{n}<p,0<m<2ab\right\}\\
=&\left\{(l,m)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\bigm|-\frac{p}{2}<l+\frac{1}{n}<\frac{p}{2},m\leq\frac{2ab}{p}(l+\frac{1}{n})+ab,l+\frac{1}{n}\leq\frac{m}{2}+\frac{p}{2}-ab,0<m<2ab,a\nmid m,b\nmid m\right\}\\
=&\left\{(l,m)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\bigm|-\frac{p}{2}\leq l<\frac{p}{2},m\leq\frac{2abl}{p}+ab,l<\frac{m}{2}+\frac{p}{2}-ab,0<m<2ab,a\nmid m,b\nmid m\right\}.\end{split}

Observe that the line
m=2abl/p+ab
contains no integer points.
This can be shown as follows:
Suppose that
(l,m)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
satisfies
m=2abl/p+ab
.
Then we have
pm=ab(2l+p)
and so
p
divides
2l
since
(p,ab)=1
.
So
m
is a multiple of
ab
, contradicting the assumption that
a\nmid m
and
b\nmid m
.
Therefore
\mathcal{S}_{2}
is indeed of the form

\mathcal{S}_{2}=\left\{(l,m)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\bigm|-\frac{p}{2}<l<\frac{p}{2},m\leq\frac{2abl}{p}+ab,l<\frac{m}{2}+\frac{p}{2}-ab,0<m<2ab,a\nmid m,b\nmid m\right\}.

See Figure
6
.

Figure 6.
The gray area indicates
\mathcal{S}_{2}
. The edges are not included.

Note that
\mathcal{S}_{1}=\mathcal{S}_{2}\cup\partial{S}_{1}
, where
\partial{S}_{1}
denotes the edge connecting
(p/2,2ab)
and
(-p/2,0)
, i.e,
\partial{S}_{1}=\{(l,m)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\mid 0<m<2a,a\nmid m,b\nmid m,l=m/2+p/2-ab\}
.

We have

\begin{split}V_{2}&=I_{2,0}+\sum_{(l,m)\in\mathcal{S}_{2}}2\pi^{2}(-1)^{m}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)e^{-\frac{2l-m+p}{p-ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}}e^{-\frac{1}{p-ab}\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\\
&\times(-1)^{pn}\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{(l-m/2+p/2)^{2}}{p-ab}-\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]\\
&\quad+\sum_{-p/2-1/n<l<p/n-1/n}(2\pi\sqrt{-1})\sqrt{\frac{ab(p-ab)\pi^{2}\sqrt{-1}}{pn}}\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{a+b-ab}\frac{\sin\left(\frac{2al\pi}{p}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2bl\pi}{p}\right)}{\sin\left(\frac{2abl\pi}{p}\right)}e^{-\frac{1}{p-ab}\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\\
&(-1)^{np-1}e^{-\frac{2l}{p}\pi\sqrt{-1}}\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{(l+p/2)^{2}}{p}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]\\
&+\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m\leq 2ab-1\\
\text{$m-p$: even}\end{subarray}}(-1)(2\pi\sqrt{-1})\frac{(-1)^{m}}{2}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\\
&\left(\pi\sqrt{-1}-2\sqrt{\frac{\pi^{2}\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\right)e^{-\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\exp\left[n\left(-ab-\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]+O(n^{-3/2}).\end{split}

6.
V_{1}
and
V_{2}

First of all note that
I_{1,0}=I_{2,0}
.
Recall that
\mathcal{S}_{1}=\mathcal{S}_{2}\cup\partial\mathcal{S}_{1}
.
Hence we have

\begin{split}&V_{1}-V_{2}\\
=&\sum_{(l,m)\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}2\pi^{2}(-1)^{m}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)e^{\frac{2l-m+p}{p-ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}}e^{-\frac{1}{p-ab}\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\\
&\quad\times(-1)^{pn}\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{(l-m/2+p/2)^{2}}{p-ab}-\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]\\
&-\sum_{(l,m)\in\mathcal{S}_{2}}2\pi^{2}(-1)^{m}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)e^{-\frac{2l-m+p}{p-ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}}e^{-\frac{1}{p-ab}\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\\
&\times(-1)^{pn}\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{(l-m/2+p/2)^{2}}{p-ab}-\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]\\
&+2\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{l}\sqrt{\frac{ab(p-ab)\pi^{2}\sqrt{-1}}{pn}}\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{ab+a+b}\frac{\sin\left(\frac{2al\pi}{p}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2bl\pi}{p}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{p}\right)}{\sin\left(\frac{2abl\pi}{p}\right)}e^{-\frac{1}{p-ab}\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\\
&\quad\times(-1)^{np-1}\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{(l+p/2)^{2}}{p}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]\\
&+2\sum_{\text{$m-p$: even}}(-1)(2\pi\sqrt{-1})\frac{(-1)^{m}}{2}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\\
&\quad\times\left(-\pi\sqrt{-1}+2\sqrt{\frac{\pi^{2}\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\right)e^{-\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\exp\left[n\left(-ab-\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]+O(n^{-3/2}).\end{split}

Since
e^{\frac{2l-m+p}{p-ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}}-e^{-\frac{2l-m+p}{p-ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}}=2\sqrt{-1}\sin\left(\frac{(2l-m+p)\pi}{p-ab}\right)
, we have

\begin{split}&V_{1}-V_{2}\\
=&\sum_{(l,m)\in\partial\mathcal{S}_{1}}2\pi^{2}(-1)^{m}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)e^{\frac{2l-m+p}{p-ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}}e^{-\frac{1}{p-ab}\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\\
&\quad\times(-1)^{pn}\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{(l-m/2+p/2)^{2}}{p-ab}-\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]\\
&+\sum_{(l,m)\in\mathcal{S}_{2}}2\pi^{2}(-1)^{m}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)2\sqrt{-1}\sin\left(\frac{(2l-m+p)\pi}{p-ab}\right)e^{-\frac{1}{p-ab}\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\\
&\quad\times(-1)^{pn}\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{(l-m/2+p/2)^{2}}{p-ab}-\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]\\
&+2\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{l}\sqrt{\frac{ab(p-ab)\pi^{2}\sqrt{-1}}{pn}}\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{ab+a+b}\frac{\sin\left(\frac{2al\pi}{p}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2bl\pi}{p}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{p}\right)}{\sin\left(\frac{2abl\pi}{p}\right)}e^{-\frac{1}{p-ab}\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\\
&\quad\times(-1)^{np-1}\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{(l+p/2)^{2}}{p}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]\\
&+2\sum_{\text{$m-p$: even}}(-1)(2\pi\sqrt{-1})\frac{(-1)^{m}}{2}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\\
&\quad\times\left(-\pi\sqrt{-1}+2\sqrt{\frac{\pi^{2}\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\right)e^{-\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\exp\left[n\left(-ab-\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]+O(n^{-3/2}).\end{split}

When
l=m/2+p/2-ab
, the summand in the first term becomes

\begin{split}&2\pi^{2}(-1)^{m}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)e^{-\frac{1}{p-ab}\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\\
&\quad\times(-1)^{pn}\exp\left[n\left(-(p-ab)-\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]\end{split}

and the summation is over all
m
such that
0<m<2ab
,
a\nmid m
,
b\nmid m
, and that
p-m
is even.
So this term cancels with a part of the last term involving
-\pi\sqrt{-1}
.
Therefore we have

\begin{split}&V_{1}-V_{2}\\
=&\sum_{(l,m)\in\mathcal{S}_{2}}2\pi^{2}(-1)^{m}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)2\sqrt{-1}\sin\left(\frac{(2l-m+p)\pi}{p-ab}\right)e^{-\frac{1}{p-ab}\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\\
&\quad\times(-1)^{pn}\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{(l-m/2+p/2)^{2}}{p-ab}-\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]\\
&+2\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{l}\sqrt{\frac{ab(p-ab)\pi^{2}\sqrt{-1}}{pn}}\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{ab+a+b}\frac{\sin\left(\frac{2al\pi}{p}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2bl\pi}{p}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{p}\right)}{\sin\left(\frac{2abl\pi}{p}\right)}e^{-\frac{1}{p-ab}\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\\
&\quad\times(-1)^{np-1}\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{(l+p/2)^{2}}{p}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]\\
&+2\sum_{\text{$m-p$: even}}(-1)(2\pi\sqrt{-1})\frac{(-1)^{m}}{2}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\\
&\quad\times\left(2\sqrt{\frac{\pi^{2}\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\right)e^{-\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\exp\left[n\left(-ab-\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]+O(n^{-3/2})\\
=&(-1)^{pn}4\pi^{2}\sqrt{-1}e^{-\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\sum_{(l,m)\in\mathcal{S}_{2}}(-1)^{m}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\sin\left(\frac{(2l-m+p)\pi}{p-ab}\right)\\
&\quad\times\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{(l-m/2+p/2)^{2}}{p-ab}-\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]\\
&+2\pi(-1)^{ab+a+b+pn}\sqrt{\frac{ab(p-ab)\pi^{2}\sqrt{-1}}{pn}}e^{-\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\\
&\quad\times\sum_{l}\frac{\sin\left(\frac{2al\pi}{p}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2bl\pi}{p}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{p}\right)}{\sin\left(\frac{2abl\pi}{p}\right)}\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{(l+p/2)^{2}}{p}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]\\
&-4\pi^{2}e^{3\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}e^{-\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\frac{(-1)^{abn}}{\sqrt{(p-ab)n}}\\
&\quad\times\sum_{\text{$m-p$: even}}(-1)^{m}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{m^{2}n}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)+O(n^{-3/2}).\end{split}

By Lemma
10.1
we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m\leq 2ab\\
\text{$m-p$: even}\end{subarray}}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)=0.

This implies that

\begin{split}&V_{1}-V_{2}\\
=&(-1)^{pn}4\pi^{2}\sqrt{-1}e^{-\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\sum_{(l,m)\in\mathcal{S}_{2}}(-1)^{m}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\sin\left(\frac{(2l-m+p)\pi}{p-ab}\right)\\
&\quad\times\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{(2l-m+p)^{2}}{4(p-ab)}-\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]\\
&+(-1)^{a+b+ab+pn}4\pi\sqrt{-1}\sqrt{\frac{ab(p-ab)\pi^{2}\sqrt{-1}}{pn}}e^{-\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\\
&\quad\times\sum_{|l|\leq(p-1)/2}\frac{\sin\left(\frac{2al\pi}{p}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2bl\pi}{p}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{p}\right)}{\sin\left(\frac{2abl\pi}{p}\right)}\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{(l+p/2)^{2}}{p}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]+O(n^{-3/2}).\end{split}

From (
11
), we have

\begin{split}&\hat{\tau}_{n}(X_{p};\exp(4\pi\sqrt{-1}/n))\\
=&\frac{e^{3\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}\sqrt{n}}{4\pi^{2}\sin(2\pi/n)}(-1)^{np+p}e^{-(p-ab+a/b+b/a-3)\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}e^{\frac{n}{4}\pi\sqrt{-1}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{ab(p-ab)}}(V_{1}-V_{2})\\
=&(-1)^{p+1}e^{-(p-ab+a/b+b/a-3)\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}e^{\frac{n}{4}\pi\sqrt{-1}}\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\sqrt{ab(p-ab)}\sin(2\pi/n)}\\
&\Biggl\{e^{\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}e^{-\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\sum_{(l,m)\in\mathcal{S}_{2}}(-1)^{m}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\sin\left(\frac{(2l-m+p)\pi}{p-ab}\right)\\
&\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{(2l-m+p)^{2}}{4(p-ab)}-\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]\\
&+\sqrt{-1}\sqrt{\frac{ab(p-ab)}{pn}}(-1)^{a+b+ab}e^{-\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{(p-ab)n}}\sum_{|l|\leq(p-1)/2}\frac{\sin\left(\frac{2al\pi}{p}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2bl\pi}{p}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{p}\right)}{\sin\left(\frac{2abl\pi}{p}\right)}\\
&\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{(l+p/2)^{2}}{p}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right]+O(n^{-3/2})\Biggr\}.\end{split}

Hence we have

(19)

\hat{\tau}_{n}(X_{p};\exp(4\pi\sqrt{-1}/n))=\frac{(-1)^{p+1}n^{3/2}}{2\pi}\left(A(n)+B(n)n^{-1/2}+O(n^{-1})\right),

where

\displaystyle A(n)

\displaystyle:=e^{\frac{n+1}{4}\pi\sqrt{-1}}\sum_{(l,m)\in\mathcal{S}_{2}}(-1)^{m}\frac{\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\sin\left(\frac{(2l-m+p)\pi}{p-ab}\right)}{\sqrt{ab(p-ab)}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{(2l-m+p)^{2}}{4(p-ab)}-\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right],

\displaystyle B(n)

\displaystyle:=2\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{a+b+ab}e^{\frac{(1-p)n}{4}\pi\sqrt{-1}}\sum_{1\leq l\leq(p-1)/2}(-1)^{l}\frac{\sin\left(\frac{2al\pi}{p}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2bl\pi}{p}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{p}\right)}{\sqrt{p}\sin\left(\frac{2abl\pi}{p}\right)}

\displaystyle\quad\times\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{l^{2}}{p}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right].

If we put
g:=2l-m+p
, then the summation range
\mathcal{S}_{2}
becomes

\begin{split}\tilde{\mathcal{S}}:=\Bigl\{(g,m)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\mid&\,0<m<2ab,\frac{p-ab}{ab}m<g<2(p-ab),\\
&\,h\equiv p-m\pmod{2},a\nmid m,b\nmid m\Bigr\},\end{split}

which is the interior of the right-angled triangle with vertices
(0,0)
,
(2(p-ab),0)
, and
(2(p-ab),2ab)
.
Using parameters
(g,m)
, we have

(20)

A(n)=\frac{e^{\frac{n+1}{4}\pi\sqrt{-1}}}{\sqrt{ab(p-ab)}}\sum_{(g,m)\in\tilde{\mathcal{S}}}G(g,m)

with

(21)

G(g,m)\\
:=(-1)^{m}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\sin\left(\frac{g\pi}{p-ab}\right)\exp\left[n\left(-\frac{g^{2}}{4(p-ab)}-\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}\right].

We have the following symmetries of
G(g,m)
:

\displaystyle G(2(p-ab)-g,m)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{ab+m+1}G(g,m),

\displaystyle G(g,2ab-m)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{ab+m}G(g,m).

We divide
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}
into three parts
\mathcal{R}^{\Delta}
,
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{R}}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\Delta}

\displaystyle:=\tilde{\mathcal{S}}\cap\{(g,m)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\mid g<p-ab\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\tilde{\mathcal{S}}\cap\{(g,m)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\mid m>ab\}

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{R}}

\displaystyle:=\tilde{\mathcal{S}}\cap\{(g,m)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\mid p-ab<g,m<ab\}

Note that we can exclude the line
g=p-ab
, since
A(n)
vanishes when
g=p-ab
.

Figure 7.
The light gray area indicates
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}=\mathcal{R}^{\Delta}\cup\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\cup\tilde{\mathcal{R}}
.
The dark gray area is
\mathcal{R}^{\nabla}
.
We put
\mathcal{R}:=\mathcal{R}^{\Delta}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\nabla}
.

Then we have

(22)

\begin{split}\sum_{(g,m)\in\tilde{\mathcal{S}}}G(g,m)&=\sum_{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}^{\Delta}\cup\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\cup\tilde{\mathcal{R}}}G(g,m)\\
&=\sum_{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}^{\Delta}}G(g,m)-\sum_{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}^{\nabla}}G(g,m)+\sum_{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}}(-1)^{ab+m+1}G(g,m),\end{split}

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\nabla}

\displaystyle:=\left\{(g,m)\mid 0<m<ab,0<g<p-ab,h<\frac{p-ab}{ab}m,h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}{p-m},a\nmid m,b\nmid m\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}

\displaystyle:=\left\{(g,m)\mid 0<m<ab,0<g<p-ab,h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}{p-m},a\nmid m,b\nmid m\right\}.

Since

\mathcal{R}^{\Delta}=\left\{(g,m)\mid 0<m<2ab,\frac{p-ab}{ab}m<g<p-ab,h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}{p-m},a\nmid m,b\nmid m\right\},

\mathcal{R}
splits into
\mathcal{R}^{\Delta}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\nabla}
.
So we can regard
A(n)
as a summation over
\mathcal{R}
.

7.
The Reidemeister torsion and the Chern–Simons invariant

7.1.
Fundamental group

We assume that
b
is odd.
For the torus knot
T(a,b)
we have

\pi_{1}(S^{3}\setminus{T(a,b)})=\langle x,y\mid x^{a}=y^{b}\rangle.

Note that

•

z:=x^{a}=y^{b}
is central,

•

\mu=x^{-c}y^{d}
is a meridian, where
c
and
d
are integers such that
ad-bc=1
, and

•

\lambda=z\mu^{-ab}
is the preferred longitude.

Put
X:=S^{3}\setminus\operatorname{Int}{N(T(a,b))}
, and
D:=D^{2}\times S^{1}
.
Let
X_{p}
be the closed three-manifold obtained from
X
by
p
-surgery.
Then
X_{p}
is
X\cup_{i_{p}}D
, where
i_{p}\colon\partial{D}\to\partial{X}
sending
\partial{D}^{2}\times\{\text{point}\}
to
\mu_{D}:=\lambda\mu^{p}
, and
\{\text{point in $\partial{D^{2}}$}\}\times S^{1}
to
\mu^{-1}
, where we identify a simple closed curve on the torus
\partial{X}
with an element in
\pi_{1}(\partial{X})
.

Since in
\pi_{1}(X_{p})
,
\mu_{D}=1
, which is equivalent to
\mu^{p-ab}=z^{-1}
, we have the following presentation of
\pi_{1}(X_{p})
:

(G)

\pi_{1}(X_{p})=\langle x,y,\mu\mid x^{a}=y^{b},\mu=x^{-c}y^{d},\mu^{p-ab}=x^{-a}\rangle.

Note that we do not need the generator
\mu
.

Regarding
X_{p}
as the Seifert fibered space
S(-a/c,b/d,p-ab)
, we have another presentation:

(23)

\pi_{1}\bigr(S(-a/c,b/d,p-ab)\bigr)=\langle\alpha,\beta,\gamma,f\mid[\alpha,f]=[\beta,f]=[\gamma,f]=\alpha^{a}f^{-c}=\beta^{b}f^{d}=\gamma^{p-ab}f=\alpha\beta\gamma=1\rangle,

where
\alpha
,
\beta
, and
\gamma
go around the singular fibers with indices
-a/b
,
b/d
, and
p-ab
respectively, and
f
is a regular fiber.
We use
\pi_{1}(X_{p})
for the presentation (
G
) and
\pi_{1}\bigr(S(-a/c,b/d,p-ab)\bigr)
for that described in (
23
).

We will construct a concrete isomorphism between
\pi_{1}(X_{p})
and
\pi_{1}\bigr(S(-a/c,b/d,p-ab)\bigr)
.

Define a homomorphism
\Phi\colon\pi_{1}\bigr(S(-a/c,b/d,p-ab)\bigr)\to\pi_{1}(X_{p})
by

\displaystyle\Phi(\alpha)

\displaystyle:=x^{-c}

\displaystyle\Phi(\beta)

\displaystyle:=y^{d},

\displaystyle\Phi(\gamma)

\displaystyle:=\mu^{-1},

\displaystyle\Phi(f)

\displaystyle:=\mu^{p-ab}.

Since we have

\displaystyle\Phi(\alpha^{a}f^{-c})

\displaystyle=x^{-ac}\mu^{-c(p-ab)}=x^{-ac}x^{ac}=1,

\displaystyle\Phi(\beta^{b}f^{d})

\displaystyle=y^{bd}\mu^{d(p-ab)}=y^{bd}x^{-ad}=1,

\displaystyle\Phi(\gamma^{p-ab}f)

\displaystyle=\mu^{ab-p}\mu^{p-ab}=1,

\displaystyle\Phi([\alpha,f])

\displaystyle=[x^{-c},\mu^{p-ab}]=[x^{-c},x^{-a}]=1,

\displaystyle\Phi([\beta,f])

\displaystyle=[y^{d},\mu^{p-ab}]=[y^{d},y^{-b}]=1,

\displaystyle\Phi([\gamma,f])

\displaystyle=[\mu^{-1},\mu^{p-ab}]=1,

\displaystyle\Phi(\alpha\beta\gamma)

\displaystyle=x^{-c}y^{d}\mu^{-1}=1,

\Phi
is well-defined.

We also define
\Psi\colon\pi_{1}(X_{p})\to\pi_{1}\bigr(S(-a/c,b/d,p-ab)\bigr)
by

\displaystyle\Psi(x)

\displaystyle:=\alpha^{b}f^{-d},

\displaystyle\Psi(y)

\displaystyle:=\beta^{a}f^{c},

\displaystyle\Phi(\mu)

\displaystyle:=\gamma^{-1}.

Since we have

\displaystyle\Psi(x^{a}y^{-b})

\displaystyle=\alpha^{ab}f^{-ad}\beta^{-ab}f^{-bc}=f^{bc}f^{-ad}f^{ad}f^{-bc}=1,

\displaystyle\Psi(\mu y^{-d}x^{c})

\displaystyle=\gamma^{-1}f^{-cd}\beta^{-ad}\alpha^{bc}f^{-cd}=\gamma^{-1}\beta^{bc}\beta^{-ad}\alpha^{bc}\alpha^{-ad}=\gamma^{-1}\beta^{-1}\alpha^{-1}=1,

\displaystyle\Psi(\mu^{p-ab}x^{a})

\displaystyle=\gamma^{ab-p}\left(\alpha^{b}f^{-d}\right)^{a}=f\alpha^{ab}f^{-ad}=f\cdot f^{bc}f^{-ad}=1,

\Psi
is also well-defined.

Since
\Phi\circ\Psi=\operatorname{Id}_{\pi_{1}(X_{p})}
and
\Psi\circ\Phi=\operatorname{Id}_{\pi_{1}\bigr(S(-a/c,b/d,p-ab)\bigr)}
, both
\Phi
and
\Psi
are isomorphisms, as desired.

7.2.
Representations

It is known that the
\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
character variety of
T(a,b)
has
(a-1)(b-1)/2
irreducible components and an Abelian component.
Put

\mathcal{P}:=\{(k,l)\mid 0<k<a,0<l<b,k\equiv l\pmod{2}\}.

Then the irreducible components are indexed by
\mathcal{P}
as follows.
Let
\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
be an irreducible representation that belongs to the component indexed by
(k,l)\in\mathcal{P}
.
Then it satisfies

(24)

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(x)

\displaystyle=2\cos\left(\frac{k\pi}{a}\right),

(25)

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(y)

\displaystyle=2\cos\left(\frac{l\pi}{b}\right).

See
[
33
,
43
]
.

The irreducible component indexed by
(k,l)\in\mathcal{P}
intersects with the Abelian component in two representations whose traces of the meridian
\mu
are
2\cos\left(\frac{(adl-bck)\pi}{ab}\right)
and
2\cos\left(\frac{(adl+bck)\pi}{ab}\right)
, where
c
and
d
are integers satisfying
ad-bc=1
.

We want to extend the representation
\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}
to a representation of
\pi_{1}(X_{p})
.
Since
\operatorname{tr}\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(x)\neq\pm 2
, we can find
Q\in\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
such that
Q^{-1}\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(x)Q=\begin{pmatrix}e^{k\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}&0\\
0&e^{-k\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}\end{pmatrix}
.
Then we have
\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(\mu_{D})=(-1)^{k}\left(\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(\mu)\right)^{p-ab}
.
Therefore if
\operatorname{tr}\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(\mu)=2\cos\left(\frac{h\pi}{p-ab}\right)
with
0<h<p-ab
and
h\equiv k\equiv l\pmod{2}
, then there exists a representation
\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}\colon\pi_{1}(X_{p})\to\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
.

Therefore we have the following proposition.

Proposition 7.1
.

Put

(26)

\mathcal{H}:=\{(h,k,l)\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\mid 0<h<p-ab,0<k<a,0<l<b,h\equiv k\equiv l\pmod{2}\}.

Then for any triple
(h,k,l)
there exists an irreducible representation
\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}\colon\pi_{1}(X_{p})\to\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
such that

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(x)

\displaystyle=2\cos\left(\frac{k\pi}{a}\right),

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(y)

\displaystyle=2\cos\left(\frac{l\pi}{b}\right),

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(\mu)

\displaystyle=2\cos\left(\frac{h\pi}{p-ab}\right).

Moreover the representation is unique up to conjugation.

We can describe these representations in terms of
\pi_{1}\bigl(S(-a/c,b/d,p-ab)\bigr)
by using the isomorphism
\Phi\colon\pi_{1}\bigl(S(-a/c,b/d,p-ab)\bigr)\to\pi_{1}(X_{p})
.
The generators of the presentation (
23
) is mapped as follows:

(27)

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=2\cos\left(\frac{ck\pi}{a}\right),

(28)

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(\beta)

\displaystyle=2\cos\left(\frac{dl\pi}{b}\right),

(29)

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(\gamma)

\displaystyle=2\cos\left(\frac{h\pi}{p-ab}\right),

(30)

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(f)

\displaystyle=2(-1)^{h}.

Remark 7.2
.

Since
X_{p}
is the Seifert fibered space
S(-a/c,b/d,p-ab)
, the result above is well known.

We also introduce reducible Abelian representations indexed by
\{l\in\mathbb{Z}\mid 0<l<p\}
.

Definition 7.3
.

For an integer with
0<l<p
, let
\tilde{\rho}_{l}^{\rm{Abel}}\colon\pi_{1}(X_{p})\to\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
be the Abelian representation sending
\mu\to\begin{pmatrix}e^{2l\pi\sqrt{-1}/p}&0\\
0&e^{-2l\pi\sqrt{-1}/p}\end{pmatrix}
.

Since
H_{1}(X_{p})=\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
, this is well-defined.

7.3.
Reidemeister torsion

In this subsection, we describe the
homological
Reidemeister torsion of
X_{p}
twisted by the adjoint action of the irreducible representation
\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}
with
(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}
.

For a representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
, let
\operatorname{Tor}_{\gamma}(X;\rho)
be the twisted Reidemeister torsion associated with a simple closed curve
\gamma\subset\partial{X}
.
Here we assume that
\rho
is
\gamma
-regular
[
53
, Définition 3.21]
.

If
M
is an oriented, closed three-manifold and
\tilde{\rho}\colon\pi_{1}(M)\to\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
is a representation.
We denote by
\operatorname{Tor}(M;\tilde{\rho})
the Reidemeister torsion twisted by the adjoint action of
\rho
.

Now we can calculate
\operatorname{Tor}(X_{p};\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}})
as follows.

Lemma 7.4
.

The Reidemeister torsion twisted by the irreducible representation
\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}
is given by

\operatorname{Tor}(X_{p};\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}})=\pm\frac{ab(p-ab)}{64\sin^{2}\left(\frac{k\pi}{a}\right)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{l\pi}{b}\right)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{h\pi}{p-ab}\right)}.

Proof.

Since
\lambda=z\mu^{-ab}
and
z
is central, by a conjugation we may assume that
\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(\mu)=\begin{pmatrix}e^{\frac{h\pi\sqrt{-1}}{p-ab}}&\ast\\
0&e^{-\frac{h\pi\sqrt{-1}}{p-ab}}\end{pmatrix}
and that
\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(\lambda)=\pm\begin{pmatrix}e^{\frac{-abh\pi\sqrt{-1}}{p-ab}}&\ast\\
0&e^{\frac{abh\pi\sqrt{-1}}{p-ab}}\end{pmatrix}
.
Therefore we have

\operatorname{Tor}_{\mu}(X;\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}})=\pm\frac{1}{ab}\frac{a^{2}b^{2}}{16\sin^{2}\left(\frac{k\pi}{a}\right)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{l\pi}{b}\right)}\\
=\pm\frac{ab}{16\sin^{2}\left(\frac{k\pi}{a}\right)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{l\pi}{b}\right)}

from (
7
).
Note that this holds for any irreducible representation in the component indexed by
(k,l)
.

Since
\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(\lambda\mu^{p})=\pm\begin{pmatrix}e^{h\pi\sqrt{-1}}&\ast\\
0&e^{-h\pi\sqrt{-1}}\end{pmatrix}=\pm\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(\mu)^{p-ab}
, we have

\operatorname{Tor}_{i_{p}(\mu_{D})}(X;\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}})=\pm(p-ab)\operatorname{Tor}_{\mu}(X;\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}})=\pm\frac{ab(p-ab)}{16\sin^{2}\left(\frac{k\pi}{a}\right)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{l\pi}{b}\right)}

from (
7
).

Since
\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(i_{p}(\lambda_{D}))=\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(\mu)^{-1}
, we have
\operatorname{tr}\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(i_{p}(\lambda_{D}))=2\cos\left(\frac{h\pi}{p-ab}\right)
.
So we finally have

\begin{split}\operatorname{Tor}(X_{p};\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}})&=\pm\frac{ab(p-ab)}{16\sin^{2}\left(\frac{k\pi}{a}\right)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{l\pi}{b}\right)}\times\frac{1}{4\cos^{2}\left(\frac{h\pi}{p-ab}\right)-4}\\
&=\pm\frac{ab(p-ab)}{64\sin^{2}\left(\frac{k\pi}{a}\right)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{l\pi}{b}\right)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{h\pi}{p-ab}\right)}\end{split}

from (
8
).
∎

Let
\rho_{l}^{\rm{Abel}}
be the reducible Abelian representation
\pi_{1}(X)\to\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
sending
\mu
to
\begin{pmatrix}e^{2l\pi\sqrt{-1}/p}&0\\
0&e^{-2l\pi\sqrt{-1}/p}\end{pmatrix}
.
Note that
\rho_{l}^{\rm{Abel}}
can be extended to
\tilde{\rho}_{l}^{\rm{Abel}}\colon\pi_{1}(X_{p})\to\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
;
\rho_{l}^{\rm{Abel}}=\tilde{\rho}_{l}^{\rm{Abel}}\Bigm|_{\pi_{1}(X)}
.
The Reidemeister torsion
\operatorname{Tor}_{\mu}(X;\rho_{l}^{\rm{Abel}})
of
X
twisted by
\rho_{l}^{\rm{Abel}}
associated with
\mu
is given by
\pm\left(\frac{\Delta(T(a,b);e^{4l\pi\sqrt{-1}/p})}{2\sinh(2l\pi\sqrt{-1}/p)}\right)^{2}
(
[
40
, Theorem 4]
,
[
64
, Theorem 1.1.2]
; see also
[
47
, Proposition 5.1]
), where
\Delta(K;t)
is the normalized Alexander polynomial of a knot
K
.

Then we show

Lemma 7.5
.

The Reidemeister torsion twisted by the Abelian representation
\rho_{l}^{\rm{Abel}}
is given by

\operatorname{Tor}(X_{p};\tilde{\rho}_{l}^{\rm{Abel}})=\pm\frac{p\sin^{2}\left(\frac{2lab\pi}{p}\right)}{16\sin^{2}\left(\frac{2la\pi}{p}\right)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{2lb\pi}{p}\right)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{2l\pi}{p}\right)}.

Proof.

Since it is well-known (see for example
[
37
, Chapter 11]
) that

\Delta(T(a,b);t)=\frac{\left(t^{ab/2}-t^{-ab/2}\right)\left(t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}\right)}{\left(t^{a/2}-t^{-a/2}\right)\left(t^{b/2}-t^{-b/2}\right)},

we have

\operatorname{Tor}_{\mu}(X;\rho_{l}^{\rm{Abel}})=\pm\frac{\sin^{2}\left(\frac{2abl\pi}{p}\right)}{4\sin^{2}\left(\frac{2al\pi}{p}\right)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{2bl\pi}{p}\right)}.

Since
\rho_{l}^{\rm{Abel}}(i_{p}(\mu_{D}))=\rho_{l}^{\rm{Abel}}(\mu)^{p}
, we have

\operatorname{Tor}_{i_{p}(\mu_{D})}(X;\rho_{l}^{\rm{Abel}})=\pm\frac{p\sin^{2}\left(\frac{2ab\pi}{p}\right)}{4\sin^{2}\left(\frac{2al\pi}{p}\right)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{2bl\pi}{p}\right)}

from (
7
).
Since we also know that
\rho_{l}^{\rm{Abel}}(i_{p}(\lambda_{D}))=\rho_{l}^{\rm{Abel}}(\mu)^{-1}
, from (
8
) we have

\begin{split}\operatorname{Tor}(X_{p};\tilde{\rho}_{l}^{\rm{Abel}})&=\pm\frac{\operatorname{Tor}_{i_{p}(\mu_{D})}(X;\rho_{l}^{\rm{Abel}})}{(\operatorname{tr}\rho_{l}^{\rm{Abel}}(i_{p}(\lambda_{D})))^{2}-4}\\
&=\pm\frac{p\sin^{2}\left(\frac{2abl\pi}{p}\right)}{4\sin^{2}\left(\frac{2al\pi}{p}\right)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{2bl\pi}{p}\right)}\times\frac{1}{4\cos^{2}\left(\frac{2l\pi}{p}\right)-4}\\
&=\pm\frac{p\sin^{2}\left(\frac{2abl\pi}{p}\right)}{16\sin^{2}\left(\frac{2al\pi}{p}\right)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{2bl\pi}{p}\right)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{2l\pi}{p}\right)},\end{split}

completing the proof
∎

7.4.
Chern-Simons invariant

In this subsection we calculate the Chern–Simons invariants of
X_{p}
associated with representations described in Subsection
7.2
.
We denote by
\operatorname{CS}(M;\rho)\in\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{Z}
the Chern–Simons invariant of a closed three-manifold associated with a representation
\rho
.
We use a formula by Kirk and Klassen
[
32
, Theorem 4.2]
(see also the first paragraph in Page 354 in that paper).
Note that in our case,
K=T(a,b)
,
E=X
,
M=X_{p}
,
\mu_{D}=\mu^{p}\lambda\in\pi_{1}(X)
and
\lambda_{D}=\mu^{-1}\in\pi_{1}(X)
.

First we consider the Abelian representations.
See
[
31
, Theorem 4.5]
, noting our sign convention (Remark
2.2
).

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
\tilde{\rho}_{l}^{\rm{Abel}}
be the Abelian representation defined in Definition
7.3
.
Then the Chern–Simons invariant of
\tilde{\rho}_{l}^{\rm{Abel}}
is given as

\operatorname{CS}(X_{p};\tilde{\rho}_{l}^{\rm{Abel}})=-\frac{l^{2}}{p}\in\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{Z}.

Proof.

Now let us consider a path of representations
\rho_{t}\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
sending
\mu
to
\begin{pmatrix}e^{2tl\pi\sqrt{-1}/p}&0\\
0&e^{-2tl\pi\sqrt{-1}/p}\end{pmatrix}
and
\lambda
to the identity matrix.
Then
\rho_{0}
is trivial and
\rho_{1}(\mu)=\tilde{\rho}_{l}^{\rm{Abel}}\Bigm|_{X}(\mu)
.
Therefore both
\rho_{0}
and
\rho_{1}
can be extended to representations
\tilde{\rho}_{0}
, which is trivial, and
\tilde{\rho}_{1}=\tilde{\rho}_{l}^{\rm{Abel}}
of
\pi_{1}(X_{p})
.
We also see that if we put
\alpha(t):=lt
and
\beta(t):=-lt/p
, then we have

\displaystyle\rho_{t}(\mu_{D})

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}\alpha(t)}&0\\
0&e^{-2\pi\sqrt{-1}\alpha(t)}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\rho_{t}(\lambda_{D})

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}\beta(t)}&0\\
0&e^{-2\pi\sqrt{-1}\beta(t)}\end{pmatrix}.

Therefore from Theorem
2.1
we have

\operatorname{CS}(X_{p};\tilde{\rho}_{l}^{\rm{Abel}})=\operatorname{CS}(X_{p};\tilde{\rho}_{1})-\operatorname{CS}(X_{p};\tilde{\rho}_{0})=2\int_{0}^{1}\frac{-l^{2}t}{p}\,dt=-\frac{l^{2}}{p},

since
\operatorname{CS}(X_{p};\tilde{\rho}_{0})=0
.
See
[
32
, Theorem 5.1]
.
∎

Next we consider the irreducible representations.
The following lemma is also well known.
See
[
32
, 5.2. Theorem]
and
[
5
, Proposition 2.3]
.
Notice again our sign convention; ours agrees with that of
[
5
]
.

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}
be the irreducible representation described in Proposition
7.1
.
Then we have

(31)

\operatorname{CS}(X_{p};\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}})=-\frac{h^{2}}{4(p-ab)}-\frac{(adl-bck)^{2}}{4ab}\in\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{Z}.

Proof.

Fix integers
c
and
d
such that
ad-bc=1
as usual.

We will define a path of representations
\varphi_{t}\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
(
0\leq t\leq 1
) as follows.

•

For
0\leq t\leq 1/2
, define

(32)

\begin{cases}\varphi_{t}(x)&:=\begin{pmatrix}e^{2(adl-bck)t\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}&0\\
0&e^{-2(adl-bck)t\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}\end{pmatrix},\\
\varphi_{t}(y)&:=\begin{pmatrix}e^{2(adl-bck)t\pi\sqrt{-1}/b}&0\\
0&e^{-2(adl-bck)t\pi\sqrt{-1}/b}\end{pmatrix}.\end{cases}

Note that
\varphi_{0}
is trivial and so it can be extended to the trivial representation
\tilde{\varphi}_{0}\colon\pi_{1}(X_{p})\to\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
.
Note also that

\begin{cases}\varphi_{1/2}(x)&=\begin{pmatrix}e^{k\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}&0\\
0&e^{-k\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}\end{pmatrix},\\
\varphi_{1/2}(y)&=\begin{pmatrix}e^{l\pi\sqrt{-1}/b}&0\\
0&e^{-l\pi\sqrt{-1}/b}\end{pmatrix}\end{cases}

since
k\equiv l\pmod{2}
.

•

For
1/2\leq t\leq 1
, we will construct a path from
\varphi_{1/2}
to
\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
.
Recall that the representation
\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}
satisfies
\operatorname{tr}\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(x)=2\cos(k\pi/a)
and
\operatorname{tr}\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(y)=2\cos(l\pi/b)
(see Proposition
7.1
).
Since the Chern–Simons invariant does not depend on conjugacy classes, we may assume that
\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(x)=\begin{pmatrix}e^{k\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}&0\\
0&e^{-k\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}\end{pmatrix}
.
Let
Q\in\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
be a matrix such that
Q^{-1}\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}(y)Q=\begin{pmatrix}e^{l\pi\sqrt{-1}/b}&0\\
0&e^{-l\pi\sqrt{-1}/b}\end{pmatrix}
.
Since
\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
is connected, there exists a path
P(t)\colon[1/2,1]\to\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
such that
P(1/2)=I_{2}
and
P(1)=Q
, where
I_{2}
is the
2\times 2
identity matrix.
Now we put

(33)

\begin{cases}\varphi_{t}(x)&:=\begin{pmatrix}e^{k\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}&0\\
0&e^{-k\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}\end{pmatrix},\\
\varphi_{t}(y)&:=P(t)\begin{pmatrix}e^{l\pi\sqrt{-1}/b}&0\\
0&e^{-l\pi\sqrt{-1}/b}\end{pmatrix}P(t)^{-1}\end{cases}

for
1/2\leq t\leq 1
.
Then since
\varphi_{t}(y)^{b}=(-1)^{l}I_{2}
and
k\equiv l\pmod{2}
, we see that
\varphi_{t}
is well defined.
Note that
\varphi_{1/2}
in (
33
) coincides with that in (
32
), and that
\varphi_{1}
equals
\rho_{k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}
, which can be extended to
\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}\colon\pi_{1}(X_{p})\to\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
for
(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}
.

Next we calculate
\varphi_{t}(\mu_{D})
and
\varphi_{t}(\lambda_{D})
.
Recall that
\mu=x^{-c}y^{d}
,
\lambda=x^{a}\mu^{-ab}
,
\mu_{D}=\lambda\mu^{p}
, and
\lambda_{D}=\mu^{-1}
.

•

For
0\leq t\leq 1/2
we see that
\varphi_{t}(\mu)
is a diagonal matrix with
(1,1)
-entry
e^{2(adl-bck)t\pi\sqrt{-1}/(ab)}
and that
\varphi_{t}(\lambda)=I_{2}
.
Therefore we have

\begin{cases}\varphi_{t}(\mu_{D})&=\begin{pmatrix}e^{2p(adl-bck)t\pi\sqrt{-1}/(ab)}&0\\
0&e^{-2p(adl-bck)t\pi\sqrt{-1}/(ab)}\end{pmatrix},\\
\varphi_{t}(\lambda_{D})&=\begin{pmatrix}e^{-2(adl-bck)t\pi\sqrt{-1}/(ab)}&0\\
0&e^{2(adl-bck)t\pi\sqrt{-1}/(ab)}\end{pmatrix},\end{cases}

and so we can put
\alpha(t):=\dfrac{p(adl-bck)}{ab}t
and
\beta(t):=\dfrac{-(adl-bck)}{ab}t
to use Theorem
2.1
.
Then we have

(34)

2\int_{0}^{1/2}\beta(t)\frac{d\,\alpha(t)}{d\,t}\,dt=-\frac{p(adl-bck)^{2}}{4a^{2}b^{2}}

•

For
1/2\leq t\leq 1
we see that
\varphi_{t}(x^{a})=(-1)^{k}I_{2}
for any
1/2\leq t\leq 1
.
So we have
\varphi_{t}(\lambda)=(-1)^{k}\varphi_{t}(\mu)^{-ab}
,
\varphi_{t}(\mu_{D})=(-1)^{k}\varphi_{t}(\mu)^{p-ab}
and
\varphi_{t}(\lambda_{D})=\varphi_{t}(\mu)^{-1}
.
Therefore if we assume that
\varphi_{t}(\mu)=\begin{pmatrix}e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}f(t)}&0\\
0&e^{-2\pi\sqrt{-1}f(t)}\end{pmatrix}
after conjugation, then we have

\begin{cases}\varphi_{t}(\mu_{D})&=\begin{pmatrix}e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}((p-ab)f(t)+k/2)}&0\\
0&e^{-2\pi\sqrt{-1}((p-ab)f(t)+k/2)}\end{pmatrix},\\
\varphi_{t}(\lambda_{D})&=\begin{pmatrix}e^{-2\pi\sqrt{-1}f(t)}&0\\
0&e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}f(t)}\end{pmatrix}.\end{cases}

Therefore we can put
\alpha(t):=(p-ab)f(t)+k/2
and
\beta(t):=-f(t)
to use Theorem
2.1
.
Then we have

(35)

\begin{split}2\int_{1/2}^{1}\beta(t)\frac{d\,\alpha(t)}{d\,t}\,dt&=-2\int_{1/2}^{1}(p-ab)f^{\prime}(t)f(t)\,dt\\
&=-(p-ab)\Bigl[f(t)^{2}\Bigr]_{1/2}^{1}\\
&=-\frac{h^{2}}{4(p-ab)}+\frac{(p-ab)(adl-bck)^{2}}{4a^{2}b^{2}}\end{split}

since
f(1)=\frac{h}{2(p-ab)}
and
f(\frac{1}{2})=\frac{adl-bck}{2ab}
.

Therefore from (
34
), (
35
) and Theorem
2.1
, we conclude that

\operatorname{CS}(X_{p};\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}})=2\int_{0}^{1}\beta(t)\frac{d\,\alpha(t)}{d\,t}dt=-\frac{h^{2}}{4(p-ab)}-\frac{(adl-bck)^{2}}{4ab},

completing the proof.
∎

Remark 7.8
.

Let us confirm that the right hand side of (
31
) does not depend on the choice of
(c,d)
as an element in
\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{Z}
.

Suppose that
c^{\prime}
and
d^{\prime}
are integers such that
ad^{\prime}-bc^{\prime}=1
.
Then we have

\begin{split}&(adl-bck)^{2}-(ad^{\prime}l-bc^{\prime}k)^{2}\\
=&a^{2}l^{2}(d^{2}-d^{\prime 2})+b^{2}k^{2}(c^{2}-c^{\prime 2})-2abkl(cd-c^{\prime}d^{\prime})\\
=&al^{2}(d+d^{\prime})b(c-c^{\prime})+bk^{2}(c+c^{\prime})a(d-d^{\prime})-2abkl(cd-c^{\prime}d^{\prime})\\
=&ab\bigl(l^{2}(d+d^{\prime})(c-c^{\prime})+k^{2}(c+c^{\prime})(d-d^{\prime})-2kl(cd-c^{\prime}d^{\prime})\bigr)\end{split}

since
a(d-d^{\prime})=b(c-c^{\prime})
.
So
\frac{(adl-bck)^{2}}{4ab}-\frac{(ad^{\prime}l-bc^{\prime}k)}{4ab}
is an integer because
k\equiv l\pmod{2}
and the right hand side of (
31
) does not depend on the choice of
c
and
d
.

8.
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{R}

In the previous subsection we show that the irreducible representations of
\pi_{1}(X_{p})
to
\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
are indexed by
\mathcal{H}
.
In this section we construct two injections
\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}
and
\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}
from
\mathcal{H}
to
\mathcal{R}
so that
\mathcal{R}=\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}(\mathcal{H})\sqcup\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}(\mathcal{H})
, where
\sqcup
means a disjoint union.

Let
a
and
b
positive coprime integers.
We assume that
b
is odd.

We first define two finite sets
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{Q}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}

\displaystyle:=\{(k,l)\mid 1\leq k\leq a-1,1\leq l\leq b-1,k\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}

\displaystyle:=\{m\in\mathbb{Z}\mid 1\leq m\leq ab-1,a\nmid m,b\nmid m\},

where
k\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l
means
k\equiv l\pmod{2}
.

Define maps
\Gamma_{+}
and
\Gamma_{-}
from
\mathcal{P}
to
\mathcal{Q}
by

\begin{split}\Gamma_{+}(k,l)&:=[adl-bck]_{ab},\\
\Gamma_{-}(k,l)&:=[-adl-bck]_{ab}.\end{split}

Here we choose integers
c
and
d
such that
ad-bc=1
, and
[x]_{p}
is the integer satisfying
0\leq[x]_{p}<p
and
x\equiv[x]_{p}\pmod{p}
.

Remark 8.1
.

By Sunzi’s theorem
[
34
]
,
\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)
is characterized as follows:

\displaystyle([\Gamma_{+}(k,l)]_{a},[\Gamma_{+}(k,l)]_{b})

\displaystyle=(k,l),

\displaystyle([\Gamma_{-}(k,l)]_{a},[\Gamma_{-}(k,l)]_{b})

\displaystyle=(k,b-l)

since
\Gamma_{+}(k,l)\equiv k\pmod{a}
and
\Gamma_{+}(k,l)\equiv l\pmod{b}
, and
\Gamma_{-}(k,l)\equiv k\pmod{a}
and
\Gamma_{-}(k,l)\equiv b-l\pmod{b}
.

We also have

(36)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{+}([m]_{a},[m]_{b})

\displaystyle=m,

(37)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{-}([m]_{a},[-m]_{b})

\displaystyle=m

for any integer
m
with
0<m<ab-1
.

Note that both
\Gamma_{+}
and
\Gamma_{-}
are injective, and that
\Gamma_{+}(\mathcal{P})\cap\Gamma_{-}(\mathcal{P})=\emptyset
.
The former follows from Sunzi’s theorem.
The latter is because
[\Gamma_{+}(k,l)]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}[\Gamma_{+}(k,l)]_{b}
but
[\Gamma_{-}(k,l)]_{a}\not\underset{(2)}{\equiv}[\Gamma_{-}(k,l)]_{b}
since
b
is odd.

Since
\#\mathcal{P}=(a-1)(b-1)/2
and
\#\mathcal{Q}=(a-1)(b-1)
, we conclude that

\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{Q}_{+}\sqcup\mathcal{Q}_{-},

where
\sqcup
denotes the disjoint union and
\mathcal{Q}_{\pm}:=\Gamma_{\pm}(\mathcal{P})
.
Note that

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{+}

\displaystyle=\left\{m\in\mathcal{Q}\Bigm|[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}[m]_{b}\right\}.

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{-}

\displaystyle=\left\{m\in\mathcal{Q}\Bigm|[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}[m]_{b}\right\}.

We also define maps
\Theta_{+}\colon\mathcal{Q}_{+}\to\mathcal{P}
and
\Theta_{-}\colon\mathcal{Q}_{-}\to\mathcal{P}
by

\Theta_{\pm}(m):=([m]_{a},[\pm m]_{b})

Then we have

\begin{split}(\Theta_{\pm}\circ\Gamma_{\pm})(k,l)&=\Theta_{\pm}([\pm adl-bck]_{ab})=([\pm adl-bck]_{a},[adl\mp bck)]_{b}=(k,l).\end{split}

We also have

\begin{split}(\Gamma_{+}\circ\Theta_{+})(m)&=\Gamma_{+}([m]_{a},[m]_{b})=m\quad\text{if $m\in\mathcal{Q}_{+}$},\\
(\Gamma_{-}\circ\Theta_{-})(m)&=\Gamma_{-}([m]_{a},[-m]_{b})=m\quad\text{if $m\in\mathcal{Q}_{-}$}\end{split}

from (
36
) and (
37
).
So
\Theta_{\pm}
is the inverse of
\Gamma_{+}
.

Example 8.2
.

When
a=2
, we have

\mathcal{P}=\{(1,l)\mid 1\leq l\leq b-1,\text{$l$: odd}\}.

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{+}

\displaystyle=\{1,3,\dots,b-2\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{-}

\displaystyle=\{b+2,b+4,\dots,2b-1\}.

We also see that
\Gamma_{+}(1,l)=l
and
\Gamma_{-}(1,l)=2b-l
, and that
\Theta_{+}(m)=(1,m)
if
m\in\mathcal{Q}_{+}
and
\Theta_{-}(m)=(1,2b-m)
if
m\in\mathcal{Q}_{-}
.

Example 8.3
.

When
(a,b)=(4,3)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}

\displaystyle=\{(1,1),(2,2),(3,1)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{+}

\displaystyle=\{1,2,7\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{-}

\displaystyle=\{5,10,11\},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{+}

\displaystyle\colon(1,1)\to 1,(2,2)\to 2,(3,1)\to 7,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{-}

\displaystyle\colon(1,1)\to 5,(2,2)\to 10,(3,1)\to 11,

and

\displaystyle\Theta_{+}\colon

\displaystyle 1\to(1,1),2\to(2,2),7\to(3,1),

\displaystyle\Theta_{-}\colon

\displaystyle 5\to(1,1),10\to(2,2),11\to(3,1).

Example 8.4
.

When
(a,b)=(3,5)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}

\displaystyle=\{(1,1),(1,3),(2,2),(2,4)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{+}

\displaystyle=\{1,2,13,14\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{-}

\displaystyle=\{4,7,8,11\},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{+}

\displaystyle\colon(1,1)\to 1,(1,3)\to 13,(2,2)\to 2,(2,4)\to 14,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{-}

\displaystyle\colon(1,1)\to 4,(1,3)\to 7,(2,2)\to 8,(2,4)\to 11,

and

\displaystyle\Theta_{+}\colon

\displaystyle 1\to(1,1),2\to(2,2),13\to(1,3),14\to(2,4),

\displaystyle\Theta_{-}\colon

\displaystyle 4\to(1,1),7\to(1,3),8\to(2,2),11\colon(2,4).

Next we construct maps
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm}
from
\mathcal{H}
to
\mathcal{R}
.

Recall the following:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}:=

\displaystyle\Bigl\{(h,k,l)\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\mid 0<h<p-ab,0<k<a,0<l<b,h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}k\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l\Bigr\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}:=

\displaystyle\Bigl\{(g,m)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\mid\,0<m<ab,0<g<p-ab,g\underset{(2)}{\equiv}p-m,a\nmid m,b\nmid m\Bigr\},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l):=

\displaystyle[\pm adl-bck]_{ab}.

We put

\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm}(h,k,l):=\begin{cases}(p-ab-h,\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l))&\text{if $\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0$},\\
(p-ab-h,ab-\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l))&\text{if $\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}0$}.\end{cases}

Note that if
a
is even, we have

\begin{split}\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h&=[\pm adl-bck]_{ab}+ab+h\\
&=[bc(-k\pm l)\pm l]_{ab}+ab+h\\
&\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0\end{split}

since
k\underset{(2)}{\equiv}{l}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}{h}
.
So in this case
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm}(h,k,l)=(p-ab-h,\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l))
.

Let us check whether the image of
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm}
is contained in
\mathcal{R}
or not.

First assume that
\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0
.
Then we have

(p-ab-h)-\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm}(h,k,l)\underset{(2)}{\equiv}p

and so the image is in
\mathcal{R}
.
Next assume that
\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}0
.
Noting that
a
should be odd, we have

(p-ab-h)-\bigl(ab-\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm}(h,k,l)\bigr)\underset{(2)}{\equiv}(p-ab-h)+(h+1)\underset{(2)}{\equiv}p.

Therefore the image of
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm}
is also contained in
\mathcal{R}
.

Since
\Gamma_{\pm}(a-k,b-l)=[\pm ad(b-l)-bc(a-k)]_{ab}=[-(\pm adl-bck)]_{ab}=ab-\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)
and
\Gamma_{+}(\mathcal{P})\cap\Gamma_{-}(\mathcal{P})=\emptyset
, we see that
\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}(\mathcal{H})\cap\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}(\mathcal{H})=\emptyset
.

We show that
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm}
is injective.
Suppose that
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm}(h,k,l)=\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm}(k^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime})
for
(h,k,l),(k^{\prime},l^{\prime},h^{\prime})\in\mathcal{H}
.
Then from the definition we have
h=h^{\prime}
.

We also have either

(i).

\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)\underset{(2)}{\equiv}ab+h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}\Gamma_{\pm}(k^{\prime},l^{\prime})
and
\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)=\Gamma_{\pm}(k^{\prime},l^{\prime})
,

(ii).

\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)\underset{(2)}{\equiv}ab+h\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}\Gamma_{\pm}(k^{\prime},l^{\prime})
and
\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)=ab-\Gamma_{\pm}(k^{\prime},l^{\prime})
,

(iii).

\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}ab+h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}\Gamma_{\pm}(k^{\prime},l^{\prime})
and
ab-\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)=\Gamma_{\pm}(k^{\prime},l^{\prime})
, or

(iv).

\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}ab+h\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}\Gamma_{\pm}(k^{\prime},l^{\prime})
and
\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)=\Gamma_{\pm}(k^{\prime},l^{\prime})
.

For the cases (i) and (iv), we have
(k,l)=(k^{\prime},l^{\prime})
from the injectivity of
\Gamma_{\pm}
.
For the case (ii), since
ab-\Gamma_{\pm}(k^{\prime},l^{\prime})=\Gamma_{\pm}(a-k^{\prime},b-l^{\prime})
, we have
b=l+l^{\prime}
, which contradicts the condition
l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l^{\prime}
.
So the case (ii) does not happen.
Similarly, the case (iii) does not happen, either.

Therefore we have
(h,k,l)=(k^{\prime},l^{\prime},h^{\prime})
and
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm}
are injective.

Noting that

\#\mathcal{H}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{4}(a-1)(b-1)(p-ab-1)&\text{when $p$ is odd,}\\
\frac{(a-1)(b-1)}{2}\lfloor\frac{p-ab-1}{2}\rfloor&\text{when $p$ is even.}\end{cases}

\#\mathcal{R}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}(a-1)(b-1)(p-ab-1)&\text{when $p$ is odd,}\\
(a-1)(b-1)\lfloor\frac{p-ab-1}{2}\rfloor&\text{when $p$ is even.}\end{cases}

we see that
\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}(\mathcal{H})\sqcup\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}(\mathcal{H})=\mathcal{R}
.

We denote by
\mathcal{R}_{+}
the image of
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm}
.

Lemma 8.5
.

We can characterize
\mathcal{R}_{+}
as follows.

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{+}=

\displaystyle\left\{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}\Bigm|[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}[m]_{b}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{-}=

\displaystyle\left\{g,m)\in\mathcal{R}\Bigm|[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}[m]_{b}\right\}.

Proof.

It is sufficient to show that
[\Gamma_{+}(k,l)]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}[\Gamma_{+}(k,l)]_{b}
, and that
[\Gamma_{-}(k,l)]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}[\Gamma_{-}(k,l)]_{b}
.

Since
\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)=[\pm adl-bck]_{ab}
and
ad-bc=1
, we have

\displaystyle[\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)]_{a}

\displaystyle=[k]_{a}=k,

\displaystyle[\Gamma_{+}(k,l)]_{b}

\displaystyle=[l]_{b}=l,

\displaystyle[\Gamma_{-}(k,l)]_{b}

\displaystyle=[-l]_{b}=b-l.

The conclusion follows since
b
is odd and
k\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l
.
∎

We define maps
\tilde{\Theta}_{+}\colon\mathcal{R}_{+}\to\mathcal{H}
and
\tilde{\Theta}_{-}\colon\mathcal{R}_{-}\to\mathcal{H}
as follows:

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}_{\pm}(g,m)

\displaystyle:=(p-ab-g,[m]_{a},[\pm m]_{b})

\displaystyle\quad\text{if $ab+m+[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0$},

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}_{\pm}(g,m)

\displaystyle:=(p-ab-g,[-m]_{a},[\mp m]_{b})

\displaystyle\quad\text{if $ab+m+[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}0$}.

Note that if
a
is even, then
[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}m
and so
ab+m+[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0
.
As a result, if
ab+m+[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}0
, then
a
is odd.

We need to check that
\tilde{\Theta}_{\pm}
is a map to
\mathcal{H}
.

Assume that
ab+m+[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0
.
Then, since
(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}
, we have

(p-ab-g)-[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}p+g+m\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0.

If
(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{+}
, then
[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}[m]_{b}
and so
\tilde{\Theta}_{+}(g,m)\in\mathcal{H}
.
If
(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{-}
, then
[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}[m]_{b}
.
Since
[-m]_{b}=b-[m]_{b}\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}[m]_{b}
,
[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}[-m]_{b}
and so
\tilde{\Theta}_{+}(g,m)\in\mathcal{H}
.

Assume that
ab+m+[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}0
.
Since
a
is odd as mentioned before, we have

(p-ab-g)-[-m]_{a}=(p-ab-g)-(a-[m]_{a})\underset{(2)}{\equiv}p+m+g\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0.

If
(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{+}
, then
[-m]_{a}=a-[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}b-[m]_{b}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}[-m]_{b}
and so
\tilde{\Theta}_{+}(g,m)\in\mathcal{H}
.
If
(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{-}
, then
[-m]_{a}=a-[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}b-[m]_{b}\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}[m]_{b}
and so
\tilde{\Theta}_{-}(g,m)\in\mathcal{H}
.
Therefore the image of
\tilde{\Theta}_{\pm}
is in
\mathcal{H}
.

Next we show that
\tilde{\Theta}_{\pm}
is the inverse of
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm}
.

When
\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0
, we have

\begin{split}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\pm}\circ\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm})(h,k,l)&=\tilde{\Theta}_{\pm}(p-ab-h,\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l))\\
&=(h,[\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)]_{a},[\pm\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)]_{b})\\
&=(h,k,l)\end{split}

since
ab+\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+[\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)]_{a}=ab+\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+k\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0
.
When
\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}0
, we have

\begin{split}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\pm}\circ\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm})(h,k,l)&=\tilde{\Theta}_{\pm}(p-ab-h,\Gamma_{\pm}(a-k,b-l))\\
&=(h,\left[-\Gamma_{\pm}(a-k,b-l)\right]_{a},\left[\mp\Gamma_{\pm}(a-k,b-l)\right]_{b})\\
&=(h,[\mp ad(b-l)+bc(a-k)]_{a},[-ad(b-l)\pm bc(a-k)]_{b})\\
&=(h,k,l).\end{split}

Here the second equality follows since
ab+\Gamma_{\pm}(a-k,b-l)+[\Gamma_{\pm}(a-k,b-l)]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+b-l\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}ab+h+b+l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0
.
Therefore
\tilde{\Theta}_{\pm}\circ\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm}
is the identity on
\mathcal{H}
.

When
(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{+}
and
ab+m+[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0
, we have

(\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}\circ\tilde{\Theta}_{+})(g,m)=\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}(p-ab-g,[m]_{a},[m]_{b}).

Since
\Gamma_{+}([m]_{a},[m]_{b})=m
from (
36
), and
m+ab+(p-ab-g)\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0
, we have

\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}(p-ab-g,[m]_{a},[m]_{b})=(g,\Gamma_{+}([m]_{a},[m]_{b}))=(g,m).

When
(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{+}
and
ab+m+[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}0
, we have

(\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}\circ\tilde{\Theta}_{+})(g,m)=\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}(p-ab-g,[-m]_{a},[-m]_{b}).

Since
\Gamma_{+}([-m]_{a},[-m]_{b})=\Gamma_{+}([ab-m]_{a},[ab-m]_{b})=ab-m
and
(ab-m)+ab+(p-ab-g)\underset{(2)}{\equiv}ab\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}0
, we have

\begin{split}\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}(p-ab-g,[-m]_{a},[-m]_{b})&=(g,ab-\Gamma_{+}([-m]_{a},[-m]_{b}))\\
&=(g,ab-\Gamma_{+}([ab-m]_{a},[ab-m]_{b}))\\
&=(g,m).\end{split}

Therefore
\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}\circ\tilde{\Theta}_{+}
is the identity on
\mathcal{R}_{+}
.

When
(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{-}
and
ab+m+[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0
, we have

(\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}\circ\tilde{\Theta}_{-})(g,m)=\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}(p-ab-g,[m]_{a},[-m]_{b}).

Since
\Gamma_{-}([m]_{a},[-m]_{b})=m
from (
37
), and
m+ab+(p-ab-g)\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0
, we have

\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}(p-ab-g,[m]_{a},[-m]_{b})=(g,\Gamma_{-}([m]_{a},[-m]_{b}))=(g,m).

When
(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{-}
and
ab+m+[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}0
, we have

(\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}\circ\tilde{\Theta}_{-})(g,m)=\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}(p-ab-g,[-m]_{a},[m]_{b}).

Since
\Gamma_{-}([-m]_{a},[m]_{b})=\Gamma_{-}([ab-m]_{a},[m-ab]_{b})=ab-m
, and
(ab-m)+ab+(p-ab-g)\underset{(2)}{\equiv}ab\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}0
, we have

\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}(p-ab-g,[-m]_{a},[m]_{b})=(g,ab-\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}([-m]_{a},[m]_{b}))=(g,m).

Therefore
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm}\circ\tilde{\Theta}_{-}
is the identity on
\mathcal{R}_{-}
.

Example 8.6
.

.
When
a=2
,
b
and
p
are odd from the assumption.
So we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=\{(h,1,l)\mid h=1,3,\dots,p-2b-2,l=1,3,\dots,b-2\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}

\displaystyle=\{(g,m)\mid g=2,4,\dots,p-2b-1,m=1,3,\dots,b-2,b+2,\dots,2b-1\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{+}

\displaystyle=\{(g,m)\mid g=2,4,\dots,p-2b-1,m=1,3,\dots,b-2\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{-}

\displaystyle=\{(g,m)\mid g=2,4,\dots,p-2b-1,m=b+2,b+4,\dots,2b-1\}.

We can put
d:=(1-b)/2
and
c:=-1
.
So we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}(h,1,l)

\displaystyle=(p-2b-h,[(1-b)l+b]_{2b})=(p-2b-h,l),

\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}(h,1,l)

\displaystyle=(p-2b-h,[-(1-b)l+b]_{2b})=(p-2b-h,2b-l),

since
l
is odd.

We also have

\tilde{\Theta}_{+}(g,m)=(p-2b-g,1,m)

when
(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{+}
and

\tilde{\Theta}_{-}(g,m)=(p-2b-g,1,2b-m)

when
(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{-}
.

Example 8.7
.

When
(a,b)=(3,5)
and
p=19
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=\{(1,1,1),(1,1,3),(2,2,2),(2,2,4),(3,1,1),(3,1,3)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}

\displaystyle=\{(1,2),(1,4),(1,8),(1,14),(2,1),(2,7),(2,11),(2,13),(3,2),(3,4),(3,8),(3,14)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{+}

\displaystyle=\{(1,2),(1,14),(2,1),(2,13),(3,2),(3,14)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{-}

\displaystyle=\{(1,4),(1,8),(2,7),(2,11),(3,4),(3,8)\}.

From Example
8.4
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}

\displaystyle\colon(1,1,1)\to(3,14),(1,1,3)\to(3,2),(2,2,2)\to(2,13),(2,2,4)\to(2,1),

\displaystyle\phantom{\colon}(3,1,1)\to(1,14),(3,1,3)\to(1,2),

\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}

\displaystyle\colon(1,1,1)\to(3,4),(1,1,3)\to(3,8),(2,2,2)\to(2,7),(2,2,4)\to(2,11),

\displaystyle\phantom{\colon}(3,1,1)\to(1,4),(3,1,3)\to(1,8),

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}_{+}

\displaystyle\colon(1,2)\to(3,1,3),(1,14)\to(3,1,1),(2,1)\to(2,2,4),(2,13)\to(2,2,2),

\displaystyle\phantom{\colon}(3,2)\to(1,1,3),(3,14)\to(1,1,1),

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}_{-}

\displaystyle\colon(1,4)\to(3,1,1),(1,8)\to(3,1,3),(2,7)\to(2,2,2),(2,11)\to(2,2,4),

\displaystyle\phantom{\colon}(3,4)\to(1,1,1),(3,8)\to(1,1,3).

9.
Topological Interpretations of
A(n)
and
B(n)

As shown in (
26
), the irreducible representations of
\pi_{1}(X_{p})
to
\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
are indexed by
\mathcal{H}
.

9.1.
Topological interpretation of
A(n)

In this subsection, we give a topological interpretation of
A(n)
.

Let
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm}\colon\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{R}_{+}
be the bijections described in the previous section.
Recall the following subsets of
\mathcal{R}
(see Figure
8
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}=

\displaystyle\left\{(g,m)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\mid 0<m<ab,0<g<p-ab,g\underset{(2)}{\equiv}{p-m},a\nmid m,b\nmid m\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\Delta}=

\displaystyle\left\{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}\Bigm|\frac{m}{ab}<\frac{g}{p-ab}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\nabla}=

\displaystyle\left\{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}\Bigm|\frac{m}{ab}>\frac{g}{p-ab}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{+}=

\displaystyle\left\{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}\Bigm|[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}[m]_{b}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{-}=

\displaystyle\left\{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}\Bigm|[m]_{a}\underset{(2)}{\not\equiv}[m]_{b}\right\}.

Figure 8.
The dark gray area is
\mathcal{R}^{\nabla}
, the light gray area is
\mathcal{R}^{\Delta}
,
and
\mathcal{R}=\mathcal{R}^{\Delta}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\nabla}
.

Put
\mathcal{R}_{\pm}^{\Delta}:=\mathcal{R}_{+}\cap\mathcal{R}^{\Delta}
,
\mathcal{R}_{\pm}^{\nabla}:=\mathcal{R}_{+}\cap\mathcal{R}^{\nabla}
,
\mathcal{H}_{\pm}^{\Delta}:=\tilde{\Theta}_{\pm}(\mathcal{R}_{\pm}^{\Delta})
and
\mathcal{H}_{\pm}^{\nabla}:=\tilde{\Theta}_{\pm}(\mathcal{R}_{\pm}^{\nabla})
.
Then we have the two decompositions of
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{R}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}\sqcup\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\nabla},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}\sqcup\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\nabla},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{+}^{\Delta}\sqcup\mathcal{R}_{+}^{\nabla}\sqcup\mathcal{R}_{-}^{\Delta}\sqcup\mathcal{R}_{-}^{\nabla}.

Remark 9.1
.

Note that
\mathcal{H}_{\pm}^{\Delta}\cap\mathcal{H}_{\pm}^{\nabla}=\emptyset
but that
\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}\cap\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}\neq\emptyset
and
\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}\cap\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}\neq\emptyset
in general.

Remark 9.2
.

Since an element
(h,k,l)
in
\mathcal{H}
belongs to
\mathcal{H}_{\pm}^{\Delta}
(
\mathcal{H}_{\pm}^{\nabla}
, respectively) if and only if
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm}(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{R}^{\Delta}
(
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm}(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{R}^{\nabla}
, respectively).
Therefore, concretely speaking, the sets
\mathcal{H}_{\pm}^{\Delta}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\pm}^{\nabla}
are given as follows.

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\pm}^{\Delta}

\displaystyle=\Bigl\{(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}\Bigm|\text{$\frac{\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)}{ab}+\frac{h}{p-ab}<1$
(if $\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0)$},

\displaystyle\phantom{=\Bigl\{(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}\Bigm|}\quad\text{$\frac{\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)}{ab}>\frac{h}{p-ab}$
(if $\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1)$}\Bigr\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\pm}^{\nabla}

\displaystyle=\Bigl\{(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}\Bigm|\text{$\frac{\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)}{ab}+\frac{h}{p-ab}>1$
(if $\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0)$},

\displaystyle\phantom{=\Bigl\{(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}\Bigm|}\quad\text{$\frac{\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)}{ab}<\frac{h}{p-ab}$
(if $\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1)$}\Bigr\}.

Example 9.3
.

Suppose that
a=2
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{+}

\displaystyle=\{(g,m)\mid h=2,4,\dots,p-2b-1,m=1,3,\dots,b-2\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{-}

\displaystyle=\{(g,m)\mid h=2,4,\dots,p-2b-1,m=b+2,b+4,\dots,2b-1\}

and so

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{+}^{\Delta}

\displaystyle=\left\{(g,m)\mid 2\leq h\leq p-2b-1,1\leq m\leq b-2,\frac{h}{p-2b}>\frac{m}{2b},h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0,m\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{-}^{\Delta}

\displaystyle=\left\{(g,m)\mid 2\leq h\leq p-2b-1,b+2\leq m\leq 2b-1,\frac{h}{p-2b}>\frac{m}{2b},h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0,m\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{+}^{\nabla}

\displaystyle=\left\{(g,m)\mid 2\leq h\leq p-2b-1,m\leq l\leq b-2,\frac{h}{p-2b}<\frac{m}{2b},h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0,m\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{-}^{\nabla}

\displaystyle=\left\{(g,m)\mid 2\leq h\leq p-2b-1,b+2\leq m\leq 2b-1,\frac{h}{p-2b}>\frac{m}{2b},h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0,m\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1\right\}.

See Figure
9
.

Figure 9.
The decomposition of
\mathcal{R}
when
a=2
(
k=1
).

Therefore from Example
8.6
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}

\displaystyle=\left\{(h,1,l)\mid 1\leq h\leq p-2b-2,1\leq l\leq b-2,\frac{h}{p-2b}+\frac{l}{2b}<1,h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1,l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\nabla}

\displaystyle=\left\{(h,1,l)\mid 1\leq h\leq p-2b-2,1\leq l\leq b-2,\frac{h}{p-2b}+\frac{l}{2b}>1,h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1,l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}

\displaystyle=\left\{(h,1,l)\mid 1\leq h\leq p-2b-2,1\leq l\leq b-2,\frac{h}{p-2b}<\frac{l}{2b},h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1,l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\nabla}

\displaystyle=\left\{(h,1,l)\mid 1\leq h\leq p-2b-2,1\leq l\leq b-2,\frac{h}{p-2b}>\frac{l}{2b},h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1,l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1\right\}

as indicated in Figures
10
.

Figure 10.
Two decompositions of
\mathcal{H}
.

Note that
\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}\subset\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}
and that
\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\nabla}\subset\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\nabla}
.

Example 9.4
.

If
(a,b)=(3,5)
and
p=19
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\Delta}

\displaystyle=\{(1,2),(2,1),(3,2),(3,4),(3,8)\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\nabla}

\displaystyle=\{(1,4),(1,8),(1,14),(2,7),(2,11),(2,13),(3,14)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{+}^{\Delta}

\displaystyle=\{(1,2),(2,1),(3,2)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{+}^{\nabla}

\displaystyle=\{(1,14),(2,13),(3,14)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{-}^{\Delta}

\displaystyle=\{(3,4),(3,8)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{-}^{\nabla}

\displaystyle=\{(1,4),(1,8),(2,7),(2,11)\},

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}

\displaystyle=\{(3,1,3),(2,2,4),(1,1,3)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\nabla}

\displaystyle=\{(3,1,1),(2,2,2),(1,1,1)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}

\displaystyle=\{(1,1,1),(1,1,3)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\nabla}

\displaystyle=\{(3,1,1),(3,1,3),(2,2,2),(2,2,4)\},

from
8.7
.

Note that
\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}\cap\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}=\{(1,1,3)\}
and that
\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\nabla}\cap\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\nabla}=\{(3,1,1),(2,2,2)\}
.

Since
\mathcal{R}=\mathcal{R}^{\Delta}\sqcup\mathcal{R}^{\nabla}
,
\mathcal{R}^{\Delta}=\mathcal{R}_{+}^{\Delta}\sqcup\mathcal{R}_{-}^{\Delta}
, and
\mathcal{R}^{\nabla}=\mathcal{R}_{+}^{\nabla}\sqcup\mathcal{R}_{-}^{\nabla}
, from (
22
) and the condition that
g\underset{(2)}{\equiv}p-m
when
(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}
, we have

\begin{split}&e^{-\frac{(n+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4}}\sqrt{ab(p-ab)}A(n)\\
=&\sum_{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}^{\Delta}}G(g,m)-\sum_{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}^{\nabla}}G(g,m)-\sum_{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}}(-1)^{ab+p+g}G(g,m)\\
=&\sum_{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{+}^{\Delta}}G(g,m)+\sum_{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{-}^{\Delta}}G(g,m)-\sum_{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{+}^{\nabla}}G(g,m)-\sum_{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{-}^{\nabla}}G(g,m)\\
&-\sum_{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{+}^{\Delta}}(-1)^{ab+p+g}G(g,m)-\sum_{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{-}^{\Delta}}(-1)^{ab+p+g}G(g,m)\\
&-\sum_{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{+}^{\nabla}}(-1)^{ab+p+g}G(g,m)-\sum_{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{-}^{\nabla}}(-1)^{ab+p+g}G(g,m)\\
=&\sum_{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{+}^{\Delta}}\left(1-(-1)^{ab+p+g}\right)G(g,m)+\sum_{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{-}^{\Delta}}\left(1-(-1)^{ab+p+g}\right)G(g,m)\\
&-\sum_{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{+}^{\nabla}}\left(1+(-1)^{ab+p+g}\right)G(g,m)-\sum_{(g,m)\in\mathcal{R}_{-}^{\nabla}}\left(1+(-1)^{ab+p+g}\right)G(g,m)\\
=&\sum_{(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}}\left(1-(-1)^{h}\right)G(\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}(h,k,l))+\sum_{(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}}\left(1-(-1)^{h}\right)G(\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}(h,k,l))\\
&-\sum_{(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\nabla}}\left(1+(-1)^{h}\right)G(\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}(h,k,l))-\sum_{(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\nabla}}\left(1+(-1)^{h}\right)G(\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}(h,k,l)),\end{split}

since
ab+p+g\underset{(2)}{\equiv}h
if
(g,m)=\tilde{\Gamma}_{\pm}(h,k,l)
.

Therefore we have

\begin{split}&e^{-\frac{(n+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4}}\sqrt{ab(p-ab)}A(n)\\
=&2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}\\
h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}k\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1\end{subarray}}G(\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}(h,k,l))+2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}\\
h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}k\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1\end{subarray}}G(\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}(h,k,l))\\
&-2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\nabla}\\
h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}k\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0\end{subarray}}G(\tilde{\Gamma}_{+}(h,k,l))-2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\nabla}\\
h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}k\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0\end{subarray}}G(\tilde{\Gamma}_{-}(h,k,l)).\end{split}

Now we define
\mathcal{CS}_{\pm}(h,k,l)
and
\mathcal{T}_{\pm}(h,k,l)
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{CS}_{\pm}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,k,l)

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}-\frac{(p-ab-h)^{2}}{4(p-ab)}-\frac{\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)^{2}}{4ab}&\quad\text{if $\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0$,}\\
-\frac{(p-ab-h)^{2}}{4(p-ab)}-\frac{(ab-\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l))^{2}}{4ab}&\quad\text{if $\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1$,}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\pm}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,k,l)

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}(-1)^{\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)}\frac{8\sin\left(\frac{\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)\pi}{b}\right)\sin\left(\frac{(p-ab-h)\pi}{p-ab}\right)}{\sqrt{ab(p-ab)}}&\quad\text{if $\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0$,}\\
(-1)^{ab-\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)}\frac{8\sin\left(\frac{(ab-\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l))\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{(ab-\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l))\pi}{b}\right)\sin\left(\frac{(p-ab-h)\pi}{p-ab}\right)}{\sqrt{ab(p-ab)}}&\quad\text{if $\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1$.}\end{cases}

Then from (
21
) we have

(38)

\begin{split}&e^{-\frac{(n+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4}}A(n)\\
=&\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}\\
h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}k\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1\end{subarray}}\mathcal{T}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,k,l)e^{n\mathcal{CS}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,k,l)\pi\sqrt{-1}}+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}\\
h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}k\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1\end{subarray}}\mathcal{T}_{-}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,k,l)e^{n\mathcal{CS}_{-}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,k,l)\pi\sqrt{-1}}\\
&-\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\nabla}\\
h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}k\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0\end{subarray}}\mathcal{T}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,k,l)e^{n\mathcal{CS}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,k,l)\pi\sqrt{-1}}-\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\nabla}\\
h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}k\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0\end{subarray}}\mathcal{T}_{-}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,k,l)e^{n\mathcal{CS}_{-}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,k,l)\pi\sqrt{-1}}.\end{split}

Lemma 9.5
.

We have

\mathcal{T}_{\pm}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,k,l)^{-2}=\left|\mathbb{T}(X_{p};\tilde{\rho}_{p-ab-h,a-k,b-l}^{\rm{Irr}})\right|.

Proof.

We will show

\mathcal{T}_{\pm}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,k,l)=\pm\sin\left(\frac{k\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{l\pi}{b}\right)\sin\left(\frac{h\pi}{p-ab}\right).

It is sufficient to prove

\sin\left(\frac{\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)\pi}{b}\right)=\pm\sin\left(\frac{k\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{l\pi}{b}\right).

Since
\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)=\pm adl-bck\equiv-bck\equiv-k\pmod{a}
and
\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)\equiv adl\equiv l\pmod{b}
, the equality above holds.
∎

Lemma 9.6
.

We have

\mathcal{CS}(h,k,l)\equiv\operatorname{CS}(X_{p};\tilde{\rho}_{p-ab-h,a-k,b-l}^{\rm{Irr}})\in\mathbb{C}/(2\mathbb{Z}).

Proof.

We will show that

(ad(b-l)-bc(a-k))^{2}\equiv\begin{cases}\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)^{2}&\pmod{2ab}\quad\text{if $\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0$,}\\
(ab-\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l))^{2}&\pmod{2ab}\quad\text{if $\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1$.}\end{cases}

•

\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0
.

Put
u_{\pm}:=\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)
.
Then there exists
v_{\pm}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\pm adl-bck=abv_{\pm}+u_{\pm}
.
So we have

\begin{split}\Gamma_{+}(k,l)^{2}-(ad(b-l)-bc(a-k))^{2}&=(adl-bck-abv_{+})^{2}-(ad(b-l)-bc(a-k))^{2}\\
&=ab(d-c-v_{+})(2adl-abd+abc-2bck-abv_{+}).\\
&\equiv ab(d-c-v_{+})(abd+abc+abv_{+})\pmod{2ab}.\end{split}

However since
abv_{+}=adl-bck-u_{+}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}(ad-bc)l+\Gamma_{+}(k,l)\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l+ab+h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}ab
, this is congruent to

ab(d-c-v_{+})(abd+abc+ab)=a^{2}b^{2}(d-c-v_{+})(d+c+1).

If
a
is even, this is congruent to
0
modulo
2ab
.
If
a
is odd, then
d+c+1
is congruent to
0
modulo
2
since
ad-bc=1
and so this is also congruent to
0
modulo
2ab
.

We also have

\begin{split}\Gamma_{-}(k,l)^{2}-(ad(b-l)-bc(a-k))^{2}&=(-adl-bck-abv_{-})^{2}-(ad(b-l)-bc(a-k))^{2}\\
&=(abd-2adl-abc-abv_{-})(-abd+abc-2bck-abv_{-})\\
&=ab(bd-2dl-bc-bv_{-})(-ad+ac-2ck-av_{-})\\
&\equiv ab(bd-bc-bv_{-})(-ad+ac-av_{-})\\
&\equiv(abd-abc-abv_{-})(-abd+abc-abv_{-})\\
&\equiv 0\pmod{2ab},\end{split}

where the last congruence follows by the same reason as above.

Therefore we conclude that
\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)^{2}\equiv(ad(b-l)-bc(a-k))^{2}\pmod{2ab}
.

•

\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1
.

Let
u_{\pm}
and
v_{\pm}
be as above.

We have

\begin{split}&(ab-\Gamma_{+}(k,l))^{2}-(ad(b-l)-bc(a-k))^{2}\\
=&(ab-adl+bck+abv_{+})^{2}-(ad(b-l)-bc(a-k))^{2}\\
=&(ab+abd-2adl-abc+2bck+abv_{+})(ab-abd+abc+abv_{+})\\
=&ab(ab+abd-2adl-abc+2bck+abv_{+})(1-d+c+v_{+})\\
=&ab(ab+abd-abc+abv_{+})(1-d+c+v_{+})\pmod{2ab}.\end{split}

Now since
abv_{+}=adl-bck-u_{+}\underset{(2)}{\equiv}ab+1
, this is congruent to

ab(ab+abd-abc+ab+1)(1-d+c+v_{+})\equiv a^{2}b^{2}(c+d+1)(1-d+c+v_{+})\pmod{2ab},

which is congruent by the same reason as above.

We also have

\begin{split}&\Gamma_{-}(k,l)^{2}-(ad(b-l)-bc(a-k))^{2}\\
=&(ab+adl+bck+abv_{-})^{2}-(ad(b-l)-bc(a-k))^{2}\\
=&(ab+abd+2bck-abc+abv_{-})(ab-abd+2adl+abc+abv_{-})\\
=&ab(a+ad+2ck-ac+av_{-})(b-bd+2dl+bc+bv_{-})\\
&\equiv ab(a+ad-ac+av_{-})(b-bd+bc+bv_{-})\\
&=ab(1+d-c+v_{-})(ab-abd+abc+abv_{-})\\
&\equiv 0\pmod{2ab}\end{split}

by the same reason as above.

Therefore we conclude that
\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)^{2}\equiv(ad(b-l)-bc(a-k))^{2}\pmod{2ab}
.

The proof is complete.
∎

From Lemmas
9.5
and
9.6
, we have topological interpretations of the terms in the right hand side of (
38
).

Example 9.7
.

Suppose that
a=2
.
If
(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}
, then
k=1
, and
l
and
h
are odd.
So the last two terms in (
38
) vanish.

Moreover, from Example
9.3
, we see that
\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}\subset\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}
and that
\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\nabla}\subset\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\nabla}
.
So (
38
) becomes

\begin{split}&e^{-\frac{(n+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4}}A(n)\\
=&\frac{1}{4}\sum_{(h,1,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}}\mathcal{T}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)e^{n\mathcal{CS}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)\pi\sqrt{-1}}+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{(h,1,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}}\mathcal{T}_{-}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)e^{n\mathcal{CS}_{-}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)\pi\sqrt{-1}}\end{split}

From Example
8.2
,
\Gamma_{+}(1,l)=l
and
\Gamma_{-}(1,l)=2b-l
.
Note that
\Gamma_{\pm}(k,l)+ab+h\equiv 0
in this case.
So we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{CS}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)

\displaystyle=-\frac{(p-2b-h)^{2}}{4(p-2b)}-\frac{l^{2}}{8b},

\displaystyle\mathcal{CS}_{-}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)

\displaystyle=-\frac{(p-2b-h)^{2}}{4(p-2b)}-\frac{(2b-l)^{2}}{8b}=\mathcal{CS}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)+\frac{b-l}{2}

from Example
8.6
.
Note that
(b-l)/2\in\mathbb{Z}
.
We also have

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)

\displaystyle=-\frac{8\sin\left(\frac{l}{2}\pi\right)\sin\left(\frac{l}{b}\pi\right)\sin\left(\frac{p-2b-h}{p-2b}\pi\right)}{\sqrt{2b(p-2b)}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{-}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)

\displaystyle=-\frac{8\sin\left(\frac{2b-l}{2}\pi\right)\sin\left(\frac{2b-l}{b}\pi\right)\sin\left(\frac{p-2b-h}{p-2b}\pi\right)}{\sqrt{2b(p-2b)}}

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{T}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l).

So we have

\begin{split}&e^{-\frac{(n+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4}}A(n)\\
=&\frac{1}{4}\sum_{(h,1,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}}\mathcal{T}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)e^{n\mathcal{CS}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)\pi\sqrt{-1}}-\frac{1}{4}\sum_{(h,1,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}}(-1)^{(b-l)/2}\mathcal{T}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)e^{n\mathcal{CS}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)\pi\sqrt{-1}}\\
=&\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(h,1,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}\\
b-l\equiv 2\pmod{4}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{T}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)e^{n\mathcal{CS}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)\pi\sqrt{-1}}+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{(h,1,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}}\mathcal{T}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)e^{n\mathcal{CS}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)\pi\sqrt{-1}}\end{split}

since
\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}\subset\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}
.

9.2.
Topological interpretation of
B(n)

In this subsection we give a topological interpretation of
B(n)
.

First note that
H_{1}(M_{p};\mathbb{Z})=\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
and so a reducible Abelian representation
\sigma_{l}
of
\pi_{1}(M_{p})
is characterized as
\operatorname{tr}\sigma_{l}=\cos(2l\pi/p)
with
1<l<(p-1)/2
.

Put

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\rm{Abel}}(l)

\displaystyle:=(-1)^{l}\frac{4\sin\left(\frac{2al\pi}{p}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2bl\pi}{p}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{p}\right)}{\sqrt{p}\sin\left(\frac{2abl\pi}{p}\right)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{CS}^{\rm{Abel}}(l)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{l^{2}}{p}.

Then we can write
B(n)
as follows:

(39)

B(n)=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{a+b+ab}e^{n(1-p)\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}\sum_{0<l<(p-1)/2}\mathcal{T}^{\rm{Abel}}(l)e^{n\mathcal{CS}^{\rm{Abel}}(l)\pi\sqrt{-1}}.

Note that

(40)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{T}^{\rm{Abel}}(l)\right)^{-2}

\displaystyle=\left|\operatorname{Tor}(X_{p};\tilde{\rho}^{\rm{Abel}}_{l})\right|,

(41)

\displaystyle\mathcal{CS}^{\rm{Abel}}(l)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{CS}(X_{p};\tilde{\rho}^{\rm{Abel}}_{l})\quad\pmod{\mathbb{Z}}.

From (
19
), (
38
), (
39
), (
40
), and Lemmas
9.5
and
9.6
we have the following theorem.

Theorem 9.8
.

The Witten–Reshetikhin-Turaev invariant of
X_{p}
evaluated at
e^{4\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}
has the following asymptotic expansion.

\hat{\tau}_{n}(X_{p};\exp(4\pi\sqrt{-1}/n))=\frac{(-1)^{p+1}n^{3/2}}{2\pi}\left(A(n)+B(n)n^{-1/2}+O(n^{-1})\right)

with

\begin{split}A(n)=&2e^{\frac{n+1}{4}\pi\sqrt{-1}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}\\
h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}k\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1\end{subarray}}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\nabla}\\
h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}k\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0\end{subarray}}\right)\mathcal{T}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,k,l)e^{n\mathcal{CS}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,k,l)\pi\sqrt{-1}}\\
&+2e^{\frac{n+1}{4}\pi\sqrt{-1}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}\\
h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}k\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1\end{subarray}}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(h,k,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\nabla}\\
h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}k\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}0\end{subarray}}\right)\mathcal{T}_{-}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,k,l)e^{n\mathcal{CS}_{-}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,k,l)\pi\sqrt{-1}}\end{split}

and

B(n)=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{a+b+ab}e^{n(1-p)\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}\sum_{0<l<(p-1)/2}\mathcal{T}^{\rm{Abel}}(l)e^{n\mathcal{CS}^{\rm{Abel}}(l)\pi\sqrt{-1}},

where
\mathcal{T}_{\pm}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,k,l)
and
\mathcal{T}^{\rm{Abel}}(l)
are related to the twisted Reidemeister torsions, and
\mathcal{CS}_{\pm}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,k,l)
and
\mathcal{CS}^{\rm{Abel}}(l)
are related to the Chern–Simons invariant as described above.

9.3.
\mathrm{SU}(2)
representations and
\mathrm{SU}(1,1)
representations

Motivated by
[
51
, Theorem 1.3]
(see Theorem
1.5
), we will study when a given irreducible representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X_{p})\to\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
is an
\mathrm{SU}(2)
or an
\mathrm{SU}(1,1)
representation.
As a result, at least in the case where
a=2
, we show that
A(n)
in Theorem
9.8
can be written in terms of these representations.

A
2\times 2
complex matrix
L
is in
\mathrm{SU}(2)
if and only if
L^{\ast}\,L=I_{2}
and
\det{L}=1
, where
L^{\ast}
is the conjugate transpose of
L
.
Note that
\mathrm{SU}(2)=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}u&v\\
-\overline{v}&\overline{u}\end{pmatrix}\Bigm|u,v\in\mathbb{C},|u|^{2}+|v|^{2}=1\right\}
, where
\overline{u}
is the complex conjugate of
u
.
A
2\times 2
complex matrix
M
is in
\mathrm{SU}(1,1)
if and only if
M^{\ast}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix}M=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix}
and
\det{M}=1
.
Note that
\mathrm{SU}(1,1)=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}u&v\\
\overline{v}&\overline{u}\end{pmatrix}\mid u,v\in\mathbb{C},|u|^{2}-|v|^{2}=1\right\}
.
The Cayley map defined by
M\mapsto\mathcal{C}M\mathcal{C}^{-1}
gives an isomorphism between
\mathrm{SU}(1,1)
and
\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{R})
, where
\mathcal{C}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}1&\sqrt{-1}\\
\sqrt{-1}&1\end{pmatrix}\in\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
.

Proposition 9.9
.

An irreducible representation
\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}\to\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
is either an
\mathrm{SU}(2)
representation or an
\mathrm{SU}(1,1)
representation.
Moreover it is an
\mathrm{SU}(2)
representation if and only if

(42)

\left(\cos\left(\frac{h\pi}{p-ab}\right)-\cos\left(\frac{(adl+bck)\pi}{ab}\right)\right)\left(\cos\left(\frac{h\pi}{p-ab}\right)-\cos\left(\frac{(adl-bck)\pi}{ab}\right)\right)>0,

and it is an
\mathrm{SU}(1,1)
representation if and only if

(43)

\left(\cos\left(\frac{h\pi}{p-ab}\right)-\cos\left(\frac{(adl+bck)\pi}{ab}\right)\right)\left(\cos\left(\frac{h\pi}{p-ab}\right)-\cos\left(\frac{(adl-bck)\pi}{ab}\right)\right)<0.

To prove the proposition, we prepare a lemma.

Lemma 9.10
.

For a matrix
M\in\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
and an integer
m
, we have

M^{n}=S_{n-1}(\operatorname{tr}{M})M-S_{n-2}(\operatorname{tr}{M})I_{2},

where
S_{n}(z)
is the
n
-th Chebyshev polynomial defined by
S_{0}(z)=1
,
S_{1}(z)=z
, and
S_{n}(z)=zS_{n-1}-S_{n-2}(z)
and
\operatorname{tr}
is the trace.

Proof.
