By the Cayley–Hamilton theorem,
M^{2}=(\operatorname{tr}{M})M-I_{2}
.
The lemma follows easily by induction.
∎

Note that
S_{n}(2\cos\theta)=\frac{\sin\bigl((n+1)\theta\bigr)}{\sin\theta}
for
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
.

Now we prove Proposition
9.9
.

Proof of Proposition
9.9
.

In this proof we use
\rho
instead of
\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}
for short.
Recall the following from Proposition
7.1
:

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}\rho(x)

\displaystyle=2\cos\left(\frac{k\pi}{a}\right),

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}\rho(y)

\displaystyle=2\cos\left(\frac{l\pi}{b}\right),

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}\rho(\mu)

\displaystyle=2\cos\left(\frac{h\pi}{p-ab}\right).

First, we show that
\rho
is a representation to
\mathrm{SU}(2)
if and only if (
42
) holds.
Suppose that the image of
\rho
is in
\mathrm{SU}(2)\subset\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
.
Since a unitary matrix is diagonalizable, we may assume that
\rho(x)=\begin{pmatrix}e^{k\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}&0\\
0&e^{-k\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}\end{pmatrix}
up to conjugation.
Note that
\rho(y)
is conjugate to
\begin{pmatrix}e^{l\pi\sqrt{-1}/b}&0\\
0&e^{-l\pi\sqrt{-1}/b}\end{pmatrix}
.
Write
\rho(y^{d})=\begin{pmatrix}u&v\\
-\overline{v}&\overline{u}\end{pmatrix}
with
|u|^{2}+|v|^{2}=1
and
v\neq 0
since
\rho
is irreducible.
Since
u+\overline{u}=\operatorname{tr}\rho(y^{d})=2\cos(dl\pi/b)
, the real part of
u
equals
\cos(dl\pi/b)
.
So we can put
u=\cos(dl\pi/b)+r\sqrt{-1}
for some
r\in\mathbb{R}
.
Now we have

\begin{split}\operatorname{tr}\rho(\mu)&=\operatorname{tr}\rho(x^{-c}y^{d})=\operatorname{tr}\left(\begin{pmatrix}e^{-ck\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}&0\\
0&e^{ck\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}u&v\\
-\overline{v}&\overline{u}\end{pmatrix}\right)=\operatorname{tr}\begin{pmatrix}ue^{-ck\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}&ve^{-ck\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}\\
-\overline{v}e^{ck\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}&\overline{u}e^{ck\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}\end{pmatrix}\\
&=(\cos(dl\pi/b)+r\sqrt{-1})(\cos(ck\pi/a)-\sin(ck\pi/a)\sqrt{-1})\\
&\quad+(\cos(dl\pi/b)-r\sqrt{-1})(\cos(ck\pi/a)+\sin(ck\pi/a)\sqrt{-1})\\
&=2\cos(dl\pi/b)\cos(ck\pi/a)+2r\sin(ck\pi/a).\end{split}

Since
\operatorname{tr}\rho(u)=2\cos\bigl(h\pi/(p-ab)\bigr)
, we obtain

(44)

r=\frac{\cos\bigl(h\pi/(p-ab)\bigr)-\cos(dl\pi/b)\cos(ck\pi/a)}{\sin(ck\pi/a)}.

Since
|u|^{2}+|v|^{2}=1
and
v\neq 0
, we have
|u|^{2}<1
and so
\cos^{2}(dl\pi/b)+r^{2}<1
.
Hence we have

\cos^{2}(dl\pi/b)+\frac{\left(\cos\bigl(h\pi/(p-ab)\bigr)-\cos(dl\pi/b)\cos(ck\pi/a)\right)^{2}}{\sin^{2}(ck\pi/a)}<1,

which means that

\begin{split}0&>-\sin^{2}(ck\pi/a)+\sin^{2}(ck\pi/a)\cos^{2}(dl\pi/b)+\left(\cos\bigl(h\pi/(p-ab)\bigr)-\cos(dl\pi/b)\cos(ck\pi/a)\right)^{2}\\
&=\left(\cos\bigl(h\pi/(p-ab)\bigr)-\cos(dl\pi/b)\cos(ck\pi/a)\right)^{2}+\sin^{2}(ck\pi/a)\sin^{2}(dl\pi/b)\\
&=\left(\cos\bigl(h\pi/(p-ab)\bigr)-\cos(dl\pi/b+ck\pi/a)\right)\left(\cos\bigl(h\pi/(p-ab)\bigr)-\cos(dl\pi/b-ck\pi/a)\right).\end{split}

Therefore if
\rho
is a representation to
\mathrm{SU}(2)
, then (
42
) holds true.

Conversely, suppose that (
42
) is satisfied.
With
r
given by (
44
) and
u:=\cos(dl\pi/b)+r\sqrt{-1}
, we have
|u|^{2}<1
.
So there exists
v\in\mathbb{C}
such that
|u|^{2}+|v|^{2}=1
.
We now show that there exists
\rho(y)\in\mathrm{SU}(2)
such that
\rho(y^{d})=\begin{pmatrix}u&v\\
-\overline{v}&\overline{u}\end{pmatrix}
.

By Lemma
9.10
, we have

(45)

\rho(y^{d})=S_{d-1}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)\rho(y)-S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)I_{2}.

Since
S_{d-1}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)=\frac{\sin(dl\pi/b)}{\sin(l\pi/b)}\neq 0
, we put

(46)

\rho(y):=\frac{1}{S_{d-1}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)}\begin{pmatrix}u+S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)&v\\
-\overline{v}&\overline{u}+S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)\end{pmatrix}.

Then from (
45
)

\rho(y^{d})=\begin{pmatrix}u+S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)&v\\
-\overline{v}&\overline{u}+S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)\end{pmatrix}-S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)I_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}u&v\\
-\overline{v}&\overline{u}\end{pmatrix}.

We need to show that the right hand side of (
46
) is an element in
\mathrm{SU}(2)
.
It will be sufficient if we can show that

|v|^{2}+\left|u+S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)\right|^{2}=\left(S_{d-1}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)\right)^{2}.

Put
\theta:=l\pi/b
.
Since
|u|^{2}+|v|^{2}=1
and
u+\overline{u}=2\cos(d\theta)
, we have

\begin{split}&|v|^{2}+\left|u+S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)\right|^{2}-\left(S_{d-1}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)\right)^{2}\\
=&|v|^{2}+\left(u+S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos\theta\bigr)\right)\left(\overline{u}+S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos\theta\bigr)\right)-\left(S_{d-1}\bigl(2\cos(\theta)\bigr)\right)^{2}\\
=&|v|^{2}+|u|^{2}+(u+\overline{u})S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos\theta\bigr)+\left(S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos\theta\bigr)\right)^{2}-\left(S_{d-1}\bigl(2\cos(\theta)\bigr)\right)^{2}\\
=&1+2\cos(d\theta)\frac{\sin\bigl((d-1)\theta\bigr)}{\sin\theta}+\left(\frac{\sin\bigl((d-1)\theta\bigr)}{\sin\theta}\right)^{2}-\left(\frac{\sin(d\theta)}{\sin\theta}\right)^{2}\\
=&1+2\cos(d\theta)\left(\frac{\sin(d\theta)}{\sin\theta}\cos\theta-\cos(d\theta)\right)+\left(\frac{\sin(d\theta)}{\sin\theta}\cos\theta-\cos(d\theta)\right)^{2}-\left(\frac{\sin(d\theta)}{\sin\theta}\right)^{2}\\
=&1-2\cos^{2}(d\theta)+\left(\frac{\sin(d\theta)}{\sin\theta}\cos(d\theta)\right)^{2}+\cos^{2}(d\theta)-\left(\frac{\sin(d\theta)}{\sin\theta}\right)^{2}\\
=&1-\cos^{2}(d\theta)-\left(\frac{\sin(d\theta)}{\sin\theta}\right)^{2}\left(1-\cos^{2}\theta\right)\\
=&0.\end{split}

Next, we show that
\rho
is a representation to
\mathrm{SU}(1,1)
if and only if (
43
) holds.

Suppose that the image of
\rho
is in
\mathrm{SU}(1,1)\subset\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
.
Since the eigevalues of
\rho(x)
are
e^{\pm k\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}
, we may assume that
\rho(x)=\begin{pmatrix}e^{k\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}&0\\
0&e^{-k\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}\end{pmatrix}
up to conjugation.
By the same reason,
\rho(y)
is conjugate to
\begin{pmatrix}e^{l\pi\sqrt{-1}/b}&0\\
0&e^{-l\pi\sqrt{-1}/b}\end{pmatrix}
.
Write
\rho(y^{d})=\begin{pmatrix}u&v\\
\overline{v}&\overline{u}\end{pmatrix}
with
|u|^{2}-|v|^{2}=1
and
v\neq 0
.
Since
u+\overline{u}=\operatorname{tr}\rho(y^{d})=2\cos(dl\pi/b)
, the real part of
u
equals
\cos(dl\pi/b)
.
So we can put
u=\cos(dl\pi/b)+r\sqrt{-1}
for some
r\in\mathbb{R}
.
Now we have

\begin{split}\operatorname{tr}\rho(u)&=\operatorname{tr}\rho(x^{-c}y^{d})=\operatorname{tr}\left(\begin{pmatrix}e^{-ck\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}&0\\
0&e^{ck\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}u&v\\
\overline{v}&\overline{u}\end{pmatrix}\right)=\operatorname{tr}\begin{pmatrix}ue^{-ck\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}&ve^{-ck\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}\\
\overline{v}e^{ck\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}&\overline{u}e^{ck\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}\end{pmatrix}\\
&=(\cos(dl\pi/b)+r\sqrt{-1})(\cos(ck\pi/a)-\sin(ck\pi/a)\sqrt{-1})\\
&\quad+(\cos(dl\pi/b)-r\sqrt{-1})(\cos(ck\pi/a)+\sin(ck\pi/a)\sqrt{-1})\\
&=2\cos(dl\pi/b)\cos(ck\pi/a)+2r\sin(ck\pi/a).\end{split}

Since
\operatorname{tr}\rho(u)=2\cos\bigl(h\pi/(p-ab)\bigr)
, we obtain

r=\frac{\cos\bigl(h\pi/(p-ab)\bigr)-\cos(dl\pi/b)\cos(ck\pi/a)}{\sin(ck\pi/a)}.

Since
|u|^{2}-|v|^{2}=1
and
v\neq 0
, we have
|u|^{2}>1
and so
\cos^{2}(dl\pi/b)+r^{2}>1
.
In the same way as above, we can prove (
43
).

Conversely, suppose that (
43
) is satisfied.
With
r
given by (
44
) and
u:=\cos(dl\pi/b)+r\sqrt{-1}
, we have
|u|^{2}>1
.
So there exists
v\in\mathbb{C}
such that
|u|^{2}-|v|^{2}=1
.
We now show that there exists
\rho(y)\in\mathrm{SU}(1,1)
such that
\rho(y^{d})=\begin{pmatrix}u&v\\
\overline{v}&\overline{u}\end{pmatrix}
.

By Lemma
9.10
, we have

(47)

\rho(y^{d})=S_{d-1}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)\rho(y)-S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)I_{2}.

Since
S_{d-1}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)=\frac{\sin(dl\pi/b)}{\sin(l\pi/b)}\neq 0
, we put

(48)

\rho(y):=\frac{1}{S_{d-1}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)}\begin{pmatrix}u+S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)&v\\
\overline{v}&\overline{u}+S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)\end{pmatrix}.

Then from (
47
)

\rho(y^{d})=\begin{pmatrix}u+S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)&v\\
\overline{v}&\overline{u}+S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)\end{pmatrix}-S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)I_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}u&v\\
\overline{v}&\overline{u}\end{pmatrix}.

We need to show that the right hand side of (
48
) is an element in
\mathrm{SU}(1,1)
.
It will be sufficient if we can show that

-|v|^{2}+\left|u+S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)\right|^{2}=\left(S_{d-1}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)\right)^{2}.

Put
\theta:=l\pi/b
.
Since
|u|^{2}-|v|^{2}=1
and
u+\overline{u}=2\cos(d\theta)
, we have

\begin{split}&-|v|^{2}+\left|u+S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)\right|^{2}-\left(S_{d-1}\bigl(2\cos(l\pi/b)\bigr)\right)^{2}\\
=&-|v|^{2}+\left(u+S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos\theta\bigr)\right)\left(\overline{u}+S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos\theta\bigr)\right)-\left(S_{d-1}\bigl(2\cos(\theta)\bigr)\right)^{2}\\
=&-|v|^{2}+|u|^{2}+(u+\overline{u})S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos\theta\bigr)+\left(S_{d-2}\bigl(2\cos\theta\bigr)\right)^{2}-\left(S_{d-1}\bigl(2\cos(\theta)\bigr)\right)^{2}\\
=&1+2\cos(d\theta)\frac{\sin\bigl((d-1)\theta\bigr)}{\sin\theta}+\left(\frac{\sin\bigl((d-1)\theta\bigr)}{\sin\theta}\right)^{2}-\left(\frac{\sin(d\theta)}{\sin\theta}\right)^{2}\\
=&1+2\cos(d\theta)\left(\frac{\sin(d\theta)}{\sin\theta}\cos\theta-\cos(d\theta)\right)+\left(\frac{\sin(d\theta)}{\sin\theta}\cos\theta-\cos(d\theta)\right)^{2}-\left(\frac{\sin(d\theta)}{\sin\theta}\right)^{2}\\
=&1-2\cos^{2}(d\theta)+\left(\frac{\sin(d\theta)}{\sin\theta}\cos(d\theta)\right)^{2}+\cos^{2}(d\theta)-\left(\frac{\sin(d\theta)}{\sin\theta}\right)^{2}\\
=&1-\cos^{2}(d\theta)-\left(\frac{\sin(d\theta)}{\sin\theta}\right)^{2}\left(1-\cos^{2}\theta\right)\\
=&0.\end{split}

∎

Example 9.11
.

When
a=2
, we can put
d:=(b+1)/2
and
c:=1
.
Since
k=1
, the representation
\tilde{\rho}_{h,1,l}^{\rm{Irr}}
is an
\mathrm{SU}(2)
representation if and only if

\begin{split}&\left(\cos\left(\frac{h\pi}{p-2b}\right)-\cos\left(\frac{\bigl((b+1)l+b\bigr)\pi}{2b}\right)\right)\left(\cos\left(\frac{h\pi}{p-2b}\right)-\cos\left(\frac{\bigl((b+1)l-b\bigr)\pi}{2b}\right)\right)\\
=&\left(\cos\left(\frac{h\pi}{p-2b}\right)-(-1)^{(l+1)/2}\cos\left(\frac{l\pi}{2b}\right)\right)\left(\cos\left(\frac{h\pi}{p-2b}\right)-(-1)^{(l-1)/2}\cos\left(\frac{l\pi}{2b}\right)\right)\\
=&\left(\cos\left(\frac{h\pi}{p-2b}\right)-\cos\left(\frac{l\pi}{2b}\right)\right)\left(\cos\left(\frac{h\pi}{p-2b}\right)+\cos\left(\frac{l\pi}{2b}\right)\right)>0.\end{split}

The second equality follows since
(l+1)/2
and
(l-1)/2
have different parities.
Since
0<\frac{h}{p-2b}<1
and
0<\frac{l}{2b}<\frac{1}{2}
, we have

\frac{h}{p-2b}<\frac{l}{2b}\quad\text{or}\quad\frac{l}{2b}+\frac{h}{p-2b}>1.

Therefore
\rho
is an irreducible
\mathrm{SU}(2)
representation if and only if the pair
(h,l)
is in the following set:

\begin{split}&\left\{(h,l)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\Bigm|1\leq h\leq p-2b-2,1\leq l\leq b-2,h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1,\frac{h}{p-2b}<\frac{l}{2b}\right\}\\
\cup&\left\{(h,l)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\Bigm|1\leq h\leq p-2b-2,1\leq l\leq b-2,h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1,\frac{l}{2b}+\frac{h}{p-2b}>1\right\}.\end{split}

Since the first set equals
\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}
from Example
9.3
, we can write
\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}
as

\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}=\{(h,1,l)\mid\text{$\tilde{\rho}_{(h,k,l)}^{\rm{Irr}}$: $\mathrm{SU}(2)$-representation},\frac{l}{2b}+\frac{h}{p-2b}<1\}

It is an
\mathrm{SL}(2;\mathbb{R})
representation if and only if

\frac{h}{p-2b}>\frac{l}{2b}\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{l}{2b}+\frac{h}{p-2b}<1.

Therefore
\rho
is an irreducible
\mathrm{SU}(1,1)
representation if and only if the pair
(h,l)
is in the following set:

\left\{(h,l)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\mid 1\leq h\leq p-2b-2,1\leq l\leq b-2,h\underset{(2)}{\equiv}l\underset{(2)}{\equiv}1,\frac{h}{p-2b}>\frac{l}{2b},\frac{l}{2b}+\frac{h}{p-2b}<1\right\},

which equals
\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}
.

Now from Example
9.7
, we have

\begin{split}&\hat{\tau}_{n}(X_{p};\exp(4\pi\sqrt{-1}/n))\\
=&\frac{e^{(n+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}n^{3/2}}{8\pi}\left(2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(h,1,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}\\
b-l\equiv 2\pmod{4}\end{subarray}}+\sum_{(h,1,l)\in\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}}\right)\mathcal{T}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)e^{n\mathcal{CS}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)\pi\sqrt{-1}}\\
&-\frac{\sqrt{-1}e^{n(1-p)\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}n}{4\pi}\sum_{0<l<(p-1)/2}\mathcal{T}^{\rm{Abel}}(l)e^{n\mathcal{CS}^{\rm{Abel}}(l)\pi\sqrt{-1}}+O(n^{1/2})\\
=&\frac{e^{(n+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}n^{3/2}}{8\pi}\left(2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{$\tilde{\rho}_{h,1,l}^{\rm{Irr}}$: $\mathrm{SU}(2)$-representation}\\
\frac{l}{2b}+\frac{h}{p-2b}<1,b-l\equiv 2\pmod{4}\end{subarray}}+\sum_{\text{$\tilde{\rho}_{h,1,l}^{\rm{Irr}}$: $\mathrm{SU}(1,1)$-representation}}\right)\mathcal{T}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)e^{n\mathcal{CS}_{+}^{\rm{Irr}}(h,1,l)\pi\sqrt{-1}}\\
&-\frac{\sqrt{-1}e^{n(1-p)\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}n}{4\pi}\sum_{0<l<(p-1)/2}\mathcal{T}^{\rm{Abel}}(l)e^{n\mathcal{CS}^{\rm{Abel}}(l)\pi\sqrt{-1}}+O(n^{1/2}).\end{split}

Example 9.12
.

When
a=4
,
b=3
, and
p=17
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=\{(1,1,1),(1,1,3),(2,2,2),(3,1,1),(3,1,3),(4,2,2)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}

\displaystyle=\{(1,1,1),(1,1,3),(2,2,2),(3,1,1),(4,2,2)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}

\displaystyle=\{(1,1,1)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\nabla}

\displaystyle=\{(3,1,3)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\nabla}

\displaystyle=\{(1,1,3),(2,2,2),(3,1,1),(3,1,3),(4,2,2)\}

We also see that
\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}
is an
\mathrm{SU}(2)
representation if and only if
(h,k,l)
is in

\{(1,1,3),(3,1,1)\},

and is an
\mathrm{SU}(1,1)
representation if and only if
(h,k,l)
is in

\{(1,1,1),(2,2,2),(3,1,3),(4,2,2)\}.

So there seems to be no good interpretation as in the case
a=2
.

Example 9.13
.

When
a=3
,
b=5
, and
p=19
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=\{(1,1,1),(1,1,3),(2,2,2),(2,2,4),(3,1,1),(3,1,3)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\Delta}

\displaystyle=\{(1,1,3),(2,2,4),(3,1,3)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\Delta}

\displaystyle=\{(1,1,1),(1,1,3),(2,2,2)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\nabla}

\displaystyle=\{(1,1,1),(2,2,2),(3,1,1)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\nabla}

\displaystyle=\{(2,2,4),(3,1,1),(3,1,3)\}

We also see that
\tilde{\rho}_{h,k,l}^{\rm{Irr}}
is an
\mathrm{SU}(2)
representation if and only if
(h,k,l)
is in

\{(1,1,3),(3,1,1)\},

and is an
\mathrm{SU}(1,1)
representation if and only if
(h,k,l)
is in

\{(1,1,1),(2,2,2),(2,2,4),(3,1,3)\}.

So there seems to be no good interpretation as in the case
a=2
, either.

10.
Lemma

In this section we prove the following lemma that we use in this paper.

Lemma 10.1
.

Suppose that
a
and
b
are coprime positive integers.
Then for any odd integer
n\geq 3
we have

(49)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m\leq 2ab\\
\text{$m$: \rm{even}}\end{subarray}}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=0,

(50)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m\leq 2ab\\
\text{$m$: \rm{odd}}\end{subarray}}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=0.

Proof.

We consider the following four cases:

(i).

(
49
) for
ab
odd,

(ii).

(
49
) for
ab
even,

(iii).

(
50
) for
ab
odd,

(iv).

(
50
) for
ab
even,

First of all, replacing
m
with
2ab-m
in the summation, we have

\sum_{0\leq m\leq 2ab}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
=\sum_{0\leq m\leq 2ab}\sin\left(\frac{(2ab-m)\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{(2ab-m)\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{(2ab-m)^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right).

However, since

\displaystyle\sin\left(\frac{(2ab-m)\pi}{a}\right)

\displaystyle=-\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right),

\displaystyle\sin\left(\frac{(2ab-m)\pi}{b}\right)

\displaystyle=-\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right),

\displaystyle\exp\left(-n\frac{(2ab-m)^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n(m-ab)}\exp\left(-n\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right),

and
n
is odd, we have

(51)

\sum_{0\leq m\leq 2ab}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
=\sum_{0\leq m\leq 2ab}(-1)^{m-ab}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right).

Therefore, if
ab
is even, then we have

\begin{split}&\sum_{0\leq m\leq 2ab}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
=&\sum_{0\leq m\leq 2ab}(-1)^{m}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m\leq 2ab\\
\text{$m$: \rm{even}}\end{subarray}}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
&-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m\leq 2ab\\
\text{$m$: \rm{odd}}\end{subarray}}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\end{split}

So we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m\leq 2ab\\
\text{$m$: \rm{odd}}\end{subarray}}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)=0,

proving (iv).
Similarly, if
ab
is odd, we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m\leq 2ab\\
\text{$m$: \rm{even}}\end{subarray}}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)=0

from (
51
), proving (i).

Next we consider the case (ii).
We assume that
ab
is even.
Putting
m=2k
, we have

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m\leq 2ab\\
\text{$m$: \rm{even}}\end{subarray}}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
=&\sum_{k=0}^{ab}\sin\left(\frac{2k\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2k\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{k^{2}}{ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right).\end{split}

We denote the right-hand side by
W_{\rm{ii}}
.
Note that

(52)

\sum_{k=1}^{ab}\left(e^{2k\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}-e^{-2k\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}\right)\left(e^{2k\pi\sqrt{-1}/b}-e^{-2k\pi\sqrt{-1}/b}\right)e^{-n\frac{k^{2}}{ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}}=-4W_{\rm{ii}}.

We will use the following Gauss sum reciprocity formula (see for example
[
8
, Chapter IX]
):

Theorem 10.2

(Cauchy–Kronecker)
.

Suppose that
c
and
d
are positive integers.
Let
w
be a rational number such that
cd+2cw\equiv 0\pmod{2}
.
Then we have

\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{k=1}^{d}e^{\frac{c}{d}(k+w)^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}}=\frac{e^{\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}}{\sqrt{c}}\sum_{l=1}^{c}e^{-\frac{d}{c}l^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}+2lw\pi\sqrt{-1}}.

From (
52
), we have

-4W_{\rm{ii}}\\
=\sum_{k=1}^{ab}\left(e^{\left(2k\frac{a+b}{ab}-n\frac{k^{2}}{ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}}-e^{\left(2k\frac{-a+b}{ab}-n\frac{k^{2}}{ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}}-e^{\left(2k\frac{a-b}{ab}-n\frac{k^{2}}{ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}}+e^{\left(2k\frac{-a-b}{ab}-n\frac{k^{2}}{ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}}\right)

Putting
c:=ab
and
d:=n
, and choose
w:=\frac{\pm{a}\pm{b}}{ab}
, we can apply Theorem
10.2
because
ab
is even.
We have

\begin{split}&-4W_{\rm{ii}}\\
=&\frac{\sqrt{ab}}{e^{\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(e^{\frac{ab}{n}(k+\frac{a+b}{ab})^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}}-e^{\frac{ab}{n}(k+\frac{-a+b}{ab})^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}}-e^{\frac{ab}{n}(k+\frac{a-b}{ab})^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}}+e^{\frac{ab}{n}(k+\frac{-a-b}{ab})^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}}\right)\\
=&\sqrt{\frac{ab}{n}}e^{-\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{abn}(abk+a+b)^{2}}-e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{abn}(abk-a+b)^{2}}-e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{abn}(abk+a-b)^{2}}+e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{abn}(abk-a-b)^{2}}\right).\end{split}

Replacing
k
with
n-k
, we see that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{abn}(abk-a-b)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n}e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{abn}(abk+a+b)^{2}}

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{abn}(abk+a-b)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n}e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{abn}(abk-a+b)^{2}}.

Therefore we have

\begin{split}&-4W_{\rm{ii}}\\
=&2\sqrt{\frac{ab}{n}}e^{-\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{abn}(abk+a+b)^{2}}-e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{abn}(abk-a+b)^{2}}\right)\\
=&2\sqrt{\frac{ab}{n}}e^{-\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}e^{\frac{a\pi\sqrt{-1}}{bn}+\frac{b\pi\sqrt{-1}}{an}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}e^{\frac{bk(ak+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\left(e^{\frac{2(ak+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{\frac{-2(ak+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\end{split}

Now let us consider the colored Jones polynomial of the torus knot
T(a,b)
.
We put

\begin{split}\tilde{J}_{k}(q)&:=q^{ab(k^{2}-1)/4}(q^{k/2}-q^{-k/2})J_{k}(T(a,b);q)\\
&=\sum_{j=-(k-1)/2}^{(k-1)/2}q^{bj(aj+1)}(q^{aj+1/2}-q^{-aj-1/2}).\end{split}

Note that this is nothing but the Kauffman bracket of
T(a,b)
with
n-1
-th Jones–Wenzl idempotent inserted, replacing
A
with
q^{-1/4}
.
Then we have

\begin{split}&\tilde{J}_{n}\left(e^{4\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
=&\sum_{j=-(n-1)/2}^{(n-1)/2}e^{4bj(aj+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{4(aj+1/2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-4(aj+1/2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
&\text{(Put $l=2j$, noting that $n$ is odd)}\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1-n\leq l\leq n-1\\
\text{$l$: even}\end{subarray}}e^{bl(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq l\leq n-1\\
\text{$l$: even}\end{subarray}}e^{bl(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq n-2\\
\text{$l$ odd}\end{subarray}}e^{b(l-n)(a(l-n)+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{2(a(l-n)+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-2(a(l-n)+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
&\text{(since $ab$ is even and $n$ is odd)}\\
=&\sum_{l=0}^{n-1}e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}bl(al+2)}\left(e^{\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}(al+1)}-e^{-\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}(al+1)}\right).\end{split}

Hence we have

\begin{split}-4W_{\rm{ii}}&=2\sqrt{\frac{ab}{n}}e^{-\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}e^{\frac{a\pi\sqrt{-1}}{bn}+\frac{b\pi\sqrt{-1}}{an}}\tilde{J}_{n}(K;e^{4\pi\sqrt{-1}/n})\\
&=2\sqrt{\frac{ab}{n}}e^{-\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}e^{\frac{a\pi\sqrt{-1}}{bn}+\frac{b\pi\sqrt{-1}}{an}}e^{ab(n^{2}-1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}-e^{-2\pi\sqrt{-1}}\right)\tilde{J}_{n}(e^{4\pi\sqrt{-1}/n})\\
&=0\end{split}

proving (ii).

We consider
\rm{(iii)}
.
Note that we are assuming that both
a
and
b
are odd.
We have

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m\leq 2ab\\
\text{$m$: \rm{odd}}\end{subarray}}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m\leq ab\\
\text{$m$: \rm{odd}}\end{subarray}}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m\leq ab\\
\text{$m$: \rm{odd}}\end{subarray}}\sin\left(\frac{(2ab-m)\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{(2ab-m)\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{(2ab-m)^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
=&2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m\leq ab\\
\text{$m$: \rm{odd}}\end{subarray}}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{m^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right).\\
=&2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k\leq ab\\
\text{$k$: \rm{even}}\end{subarray}}\sin\left(\frac{(ab-k)\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{(ab-k)\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{(ab-k)^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
=&2\exp\left(\frac{-abn\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k\leq ab\\
\text{$k$: \rm{even}}\end{subarray}}\sqrt{-1}^{k}\sin\left(\frac{k\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{k\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{k^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right).\end{split}

On the other hand, since we have

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k\leq 2ab\\
\text{$k$: \rm{even}}\end{subarray}}\sqrt{-1}^{k}\sin\left(\frac{k\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{k\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{k^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k\leq ab\\
\text{$k$: \rm{even}}\end{subarray}}\sqrt{-1}^{k}\sin\left(\frac{k\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{k\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{k^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k\leq ab\\
\text{$k$: \rm{even}}\end{subarray}}\sqrt{-1}^{2ab-k}\sin\left(\frac{(2ab-k)\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{(2ab-k)\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{(2ab-k)^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
=&2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k\leq ab\\
\text{$k$: \rm{even}}\end{subarray}}\sqrt{-1}^{k}\sin\left(\frac{k\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{k\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{k^{2}}{4ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
=&2\sum_{l=0}^{ab}(-1)^{l}\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{l^{2}}{ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right),\end{split}

(
50
) becomes

\exp\left(\frac{-abn\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4}\right)\sum_{l=0}^{ab}(-1)^{l}\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{l^{2}}{ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right).

In a similar way, the summation above becomes

\begin{split}&\sum_{l=0}^{ab}(-1)^{l}\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{l^{2}}{ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq l\leq ab\\
\text{$l$: even}\end{subarray}}\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{l^{2}}{ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
&-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq l\leq ab\\
\text{$l$: even}\end{subarray}}\sin\left(\frac{2(ab-l)\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2(ab-l)\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{(ab-l)^{2}}{ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
&\text{(since $ab$ and $n$ are odd)}\\
=&2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq l\leq ab\\
\text{$l$: even}\end{subarray}}\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{l^{2}}{ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq l\leq ab\\
\text{$l$: even}\end{subarray}}\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{l^{2}}{ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq l\leq ab\\
\text{$l$: even}\end{subarray}}\sin\left(\frac{2(2ab-l)\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2(2ab-l)\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{(2ab-l)^{2}}{ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq l\leq 2ab\\
\text{$l$: even}\end{subarray}}\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2l\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-n\frac{l^{2}}{ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)\\
=&\sum_{h=0}^{ab}\sin\left(\frac{4h\pi}{a}\right)\sin\left(\frac{4h\pi}{b}\right)\exp\left(-4n\frac{h^{2}}{ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}\right).\end{split}

We denote the right-hand side by
W_{\rm{iii}}
, and show that it vanishes.

Using the equality

\sum_{k=1}^{ab}\left(e^{4k\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}-e^{4k\pi\sqrt{-1}/a}\right)\left(e^{4k\pi\sqrt{-1}/b}-e^{4k\pi\sqrt{-1}/b}\right)e^{-4n\frac{k^{2}}{ab}\pi\sqrt{-1}}=-4W_{\rm{iii}},

we apply the Gauss sum reciprocity formula.
Putting
c:=ab
,
d:=4n
, and
w:=\frac{2(\pm a\pm b)}{ab}
in Theorem
10.2
, we have

\begin{split}&-4W_{\rm{iii}}\\
=&\sum_{k=1}^{ab}\left(e^{\left(4k\frac{a+b}{ab}-4n\frac{k^{2}}{ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}}-e^{\left(4k\frac{-a+b}{ab}-4n\frac{k^{2}}{ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}}-e^{\left(4k\frac{a-b}{ab}-4n\frac{k^{2}}{ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}}+e^{\left(4k\frac{-a-b}{ab}-4n\frac{k^{2}}{ab}\right)\pi\sqrt{-1}}\right)\\
=&\frac{\sqrt{ab}}{e^{\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}\sqrt{4n}}\\
&\times\sum_{l=1}^{4n}\left(e^{\frac{ab}{4n}(l+\frac{2(a+b)}{ab})^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}}-e^{\frac{ab}{4n}(l+\frac{2(-a+b)}{ab})^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}}-e^{\frac{ab}{4n}(l+\frac{2(a-b)}{ab})^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}}+e^{\frac{ab}{4n}(l+\frac{2(-a-b)}{ab})^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}}\right)\\
=&\sqrt{\frac{ab}{4n}}e^{-\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}\\
&\times\sum_{l=1}^{4n}\left(e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4abn}(abl+2a+2b)^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}}-e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4abn}(abl-2a+2b)^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}}-e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4abn}(abl+2a-2b)^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}}+e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4abn}(abl-2a-2b)^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}}\right).\end{split}

Replacing
l
with
4n-l
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{4n}e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4abn}(abl-2a-2b)^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{4n}e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4abn}(abl+2a+2b)^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}},

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{4n}e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4abn}(abl+2a-2b)^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{4n}e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4abn}(abl+2a-2b)^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}}.

Therefore we have

\begin{split}-4W_{\rm{iii}}&=\sqrt{\frac{ab}{n}}e^{-\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}\sum_{l=1}^{4n}\left(e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4abn}(abl+2a+2b)^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}}-e^{\frac{\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4abn}(abl-2a+2b)^{2}\pi\sqrt{-1}}\right).\\
=&\sqrt{\frac{ab}{n}}e^{-\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}e^{\frac{a\pi\sqrt{-1}}{bn}+\frac{b\pi\sqrt{-1}}{an}}\sum_{l=1}^{4n}e^{\frac{bl(al+4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right).\end{split}

We denote the summation in the right-hand side by
\tilde{W}_{\rm{iii}}
.
Note that

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq 4n\\
\text{$l$: even}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{bl(al+4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq 2n\\
\text{$l$: even}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{bl(al+4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq 2n\\
\text{$l$: even}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{b(l+2n)(a(l+2n)+4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{\frac{(a(l+2n)+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{(a(l+2n)+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq 2n\\
\text{$l$: even}\end{subarray}}\left(1+(-1)^{b(2+al+an)}\right)e^{\frac{bl(al+4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
=&0.\end{split}

In a similar way we can prove

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq 4n\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{bl(al+4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq 2n\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}\left(1+(-1)^{b(2+al+an)}\right)e^{\frac{bl(al+4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
=&2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq 2n\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{bl(al+4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right).\end{split}

Hence we have the following four equalities:

(53)

\tilde{W}_{\rm{iii}}=2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq 2n\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{bl(al+4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right),

(54)

\begin{split}\tilde{W}_{\rm{iii}}&=2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq 2n\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{b(2n-l)(a(2n-l)+4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}(e^{\frac{(a(2n-l)+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{\frac{-(a(2n-l)+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}})\\
&=2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq 2n\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{bl(al-4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}(e^{\frac{-(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{\frac{(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}),\end{split}

(55)

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n+1\leq l\leq 2n-1\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{bl(al+4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq n-1\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{b(2n-l)(a(2n-l)+4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{\frac{(a(2n-l)+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{(a(2n-l)+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq n-1\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{bl(al-4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{-\frac{(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{\frac{(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right),\end{split}

(56)

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n+1\leq l\leq 2n-1\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{bl(al-4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{-\frac{(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{\frac{(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq n-1\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{b(2n-l)(a(2n-l)-4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{-\frac{(a(2n-l)-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{\frac{(a(2n-l)-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq n-1\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{bl(al+4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\end{split}

since
n
is odd.

Add (
53
) and (
54
), and divide it by two, we have

(57)

\begin{split}\tilde{W}_{\rm{iii}}&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq 2n\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{bl(al+4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
&\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq 2n\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{bl(al-4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{-\frac{(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{\frac{(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right).\end{split}

From (
55
), the first summation in the right-hand side of (
57
) becomes

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq n-1\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{bl(al+4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
+e^{\frac{bn(an-4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{\frac{(an-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{(an-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq n-1\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{bl(al-4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{\frac{(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right).

From (
56
), the second summation in the right-hand side of (
57
) becomes

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq n-1\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{bl(al-4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{-\frac{(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{\frac{(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
+e^{\frac{bn(an-4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{-\frac{(an-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{\frac{(an-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq n-1\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{bl(al+4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{-\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right).

Therefore (
57
) turns out to be

\begin{split}\tilde{W}_{\rm{iii}}&=2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq n-1\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{bl(al+4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
&\quad+2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq n-1\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{bl(al-4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{-\frac{(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{\frac{(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
&\quad-2e^{abn\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}\left(e^{\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
&=2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq n-1\\
\text{$l$: even}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{b(n-l)(a(n-l)+4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{\frac{(a(n-l)+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{(a(n-l)+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
&\quad+2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq n-1\\
\text{$l$: even}\end{subarray}}e^{\frac{b(n-l)(a(n-l)-4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{-\frac{(a(n-l)-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{\frac{(a(n-l)-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
&\quad-2e^{abn\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}\left(e^{\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
&=2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq n-1\\
\text{$l$: even}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{l/2}e^{abn\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}e^{\frac{bl(al-4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{-\frac{(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{\frac{(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
&\quad+2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq n-1\\
\text{$l$: even}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{l/2}e^{abn\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}e^{\frac{bl(al+4)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}\left(e^{\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
&\quad-2e^{abn\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}\left(e^{\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
&=2e^{abn\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}\sum_{l=1}^{(n-1)/2}(-1)^{l}e^{\frac{bl(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\left(e^{-\frac{2(al-1)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{\frac{2(al-1)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
&\quad+2e^{abn\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}\sum_{l=1}^{(n-1)/2}(-1)^{l}e^{\frac{bl(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\left(e^{\frac{2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-2\frac{(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
&\quad-2e^{abn\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}\left(e^{\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}-e^{-\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}\right).\end{split}

Now we will calculate
\tilde{J}_{n}\left(e^{4\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}\right)
.
Since
ab
is odd, we have

\begin{split}\tilde{J}_{n}\left(e^{4\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)=&\sum_{j=-(n-1)/2}^{(n-1)/2}e^{4bj(aj+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{4(aj+1/2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-4(aj+1/2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
&\text{(Put $l=2j$, noting that $n$ is odd)}\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1-n\leq l\leq n-1\\
\text{$l$: even}\end{subarray}}e^{bl(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq l\leq n-1\\
\text{$l$: even}\end{subarray}}e^{bl(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
&\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq n-2\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{b(l-n)(a(l-n)+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{2(a(l-n)+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-2(a(l-n)+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
&\text{(since $ab$ and $n$ are odd)}\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq l\leq n-1\\
\text{$l$: even}\end{subarray}}e^{bl(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
&-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq n-2\\
\text{$l$: odd}\end{subarray}}e^{bl(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
=&\sum_{l=0}^{n-1}(-1)^{l}e^{bl(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right).\end{split}

Since we have

\begin{split}&\sum_{l=0}^{n-1}(-1)^{l}e^{bl(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
=&\sum_{l=1}^{n}(-1)^{n-l}e^{b(n-l)(a(n-l)+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{2(a(n-l)+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-2(a(n-l)+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
=&\sum_{l=0}^{n-1}(-1)^{l}e^{bl(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{-2(al-1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{2(al-1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right),\end{split}

we conclude that

(58)

\begin{split}\tilde{J}_{n}\left(e^{4\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)=&\frac{1}{2}\sum_{l=0}^{n-1}(-1)^{l}e^{bl(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{l=0}^{n-1}(-1)^{l}e^{bl(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{-2(al-1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{2(al-1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right).\end{split}

Now we have

(59)

\begin{split}&\sum_{l=0}^{n-1}(-1)^{l}e^{bl(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
=&\sum_{l=0}^{(n-1)/2}(-1)^{l}e^{bl(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
&+\sum_{l=1}^{(n-1)/2}(-1)^{n-l}e^{b(n-l)(a(n-l)+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{2(a(n-l)+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-2(a(n-l)+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
=&\sum_{l=0}^{(n-1)/2}(-1)^{l}e^{bl(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
&+\sum_{l=1}^{(n-1)/2}(-1)^{l}e^{bl(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{-2(al-1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{2(al-1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\end{split}

and

(60)

\begin{split}&\sum_{l=0}^{n-1}(-1)^{l}e^{bl(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{-2(al-1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{2(al-1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
=&\sum_{l=0}^{(n-1)/2}(-1)^{l}e^{bl(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{-2(al-1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{2(al-1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
&+\sum_{l=1}^{(n-1)/2}(-1)^{l}e^{bl(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right).\end{split}

Adding (
59
) and (
60
), and dividing it by two, we obtain from (
58
)

\begin{split}\tilde{J}_{n}\left(e^{4\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)&=\sum_{l=0}^{(n-1)/2}(-1)^{l}e^{bl(al+2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-2(al+1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
&\quad+\sum_{l=0}^{(n-1)/2}(-1)^{l}e^{bl(al-2)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\left(e^{-2(al-1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{2(al-1)\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)\\
&\quad-\left(e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}-e^{-2\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right).\end{split}

Therefore we finally have

\tilde{W}_{\rm{iii}}=2e^{abn\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}\widetilde{J}_{n}\left(e^{4\pi\sqrt{-1}/n}\right)=0

and

-4W_{\rm{iii}}=\sqrt{\frac{ab}{n}}e^{-\pi\sqrt{-1}/4}e^{\frac{a\pi\sqrt{-1}}{bn}+\frac{b\pi\sqrt{-1}}{an}}\tilde{W}_{\rm{iii}}=0.

This completes the proof.
∎
